## Release 5.80: tag patterns and new tagged labels (2026-11-06)

The tagged labels plan's stage 31, items 106 and 107 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/tagged-labels-plan.md`, decisions 372 to 375 and 377, taken as recommended; the mock, `docs/browse-tool/mockup/tagged.js`, approved 2026-11-05). The owner asked: "many of our plant instrument tags are titled XXX###. I'd want to be able to define that pattern and have new content tagged with whatever new undefined inputs match that pattern." No schema change: 5.79's `tagged_labels` already had how a label came (`pattern`), the pattern's name, and `glossary` or `suggested`. No new package.

- **Masks (`docrepo/tagged_masks.py`, decision 372).** `X` a capital letter, `#` a digit, `*` either, `[ ]` optional, `' '` itself (`'XV'-####`), a hyphen, underscore or space where a new label's spelling has that mark, and any other letter, a digit, `/` or `.` itself. Marks are never required in the text: `XXX###` finds FIC221, FIC-221 and FIC_221 and spells each FIC221; wherever letters meet digits a mark may stand or not. A match is spelled as its mask is, each part between the mask's marks joined by them. DocRepo makes each mask a regular expression of its own, with no repetition and at most 4 optional parts in at most 32 characters, so trying it at one place takes a bounded number of steps; the owner never writes one. A mask that can't be used says why ("A [ isn't closed.", "A mask needs a digit (#) outside brackets, or it would match ordinary words.").
- **Allow a space** (**Space** on each pattern), off by default: a space may then stand between any of its parts. A space typed in the mask may stand there whatever the tick.
- **Settings' Tagged labels section (`docrepo/ui/tagged_settings.py`, a form of its own as release 5.95's rule asks):** a row per pattern (its name, its mask in the mono font, Space, what it means as it's typed, "Such as FT-2041 to FTIC-2041", or why it can't be used, and ×), **Add a pattern**, and five made-up examples to start from (`tagged_masks.EXAMPLES`): DocRepo ships none. Then **Never a tag**, the tester and **Try on everything**. Save settings checks every mask and entry, saying which is wrong (`settings_view.check_tagged`; each pattern needs a name of its own, its mask when none is typed), and says how many new tagged labels everything now has.
- **Kept in `Config\settings.json`** (`tagged_patterns`, `tagged_never`, through `config.py`, written once set). docs.db keeps a copy in `app_state` (`tagged.set_patterns`), which a save's matching reads, as it has no data root. It's made again from settings.json at each start (`app.run`) and before the `rebuild` command, so a settings.json changed by hand is used; a pattern with no mask, or one that can't be compiled, is left out with a line in the log.
- **New tagged labels at once (decision 374).** What a pattern finds that has no glossary label with its letters and digits becomes a **suggested** row in `tagged_labels` (how it came `pattern`, the pattern's name, when), put on every capture, document and notes page that mentions it when it's saved, and on a task when its own fields are written (`tasks.create_task` and `update_task` call `tagged.index_task`). Its chip is marked **new**, in the caution colours with "new" after it, with "New: found by the Instrument tag pattern, and not in the glossary yet" on hover; a task's Details and Task.md list it like any other. A glossary label is always found first; a suggested one is found only by a pattern, so it isn't matched in lower case until it's added to the glossary. A suggested label nothing mentions any more is dropped when everything is matched again, unless the owner added it to an item by hand.
- **The Suggested list (`docrepo/ui/lists/tagged.py`).** The Tagged labels view lists the suggestions first, under a **Suggested** heading, the most mentioned first, each with "From Instrument tag · in Reactor upgrade and 2 more" (its top-level tasks, the most first, and "not filed yet"), then the glossary under its own heading. Opening the view selects the most mentioned. **Patterns…** opens Settings at the section.
- **A suggestion's page** ("Tagged label · suggested"): how it's found ("New: found by the Instrument tag pattern, spelled as its mask is, and not in the glossary"), how it's written, and everything that mentions it, with **Add to glossary…** (a description and aliases; it's then an ordinary label, found however it's written, "Found by the Instrument tag pattern, then added to the glossary"), **Same as…** (a glossary label picked or typed however it's written: the suggestion becomes its alias, and its mentions and the owner's own changes on it move there) and **Not a tag** (the owner's own changes on it go, it goes on Never a tag in settings.json, and matching again removes it everywhere; Undo in the status line takes it off the list).
- **Never a tag (decision 375):** whole tags (ABC123) or starts with `*` (`API*`), compared by their letters and digits, typed in Settings separated by commas, or filled by Not a tag. It applies to patterns only: a glossary label is always found.
- **A pair is both tags for a pattern too (decision 370),** as the owner asked on 2026-11-07: "P-204A/B" that `X[X]-###[X]` finds is P-204A and P-204B, each new (or the glossary's, or on Never a tag) on its own, the second's hit the "/B" as a glossary label's is. Only when the mask allows the other letter where the first was (`XX-##'A'` finds only TK-12A in "TK-12A/B"), and each capital after a slash: "P-204A/B/C" is three, for a glossary label too (it was two until then). The kept words already join across the slash, so nothing is read again.
- **The tester (decision 373).** **Try it**, a box to paste text into, marks what the glossary and the patterns as they stand in the form find, 150 ms after each key: a glossary label green, an alias and a word alias (underlined) blue, a new one in the caution colour, one on Never a tag struck through, with a legend and "Found: FIC-2210, TIC-2216 (new, Instrument tag)". **Try on everything** runs the patterns as they stand, before they're saved, over every live capture, document, notes page and task from the kept words, and says "3 new, in 2 items: API-6100 (2), ABC-1234 (1), TT-2215 (1)", then each with its count, pattern and spellings, and **Not a tag**, which puts it on the form's Never a tag and tries again. Neither writes anything.
- **Kept current (decision 377).** A pattern change matches every item's kept words again (`rematch`), as a glossary change does: no file is read (a test makes reading one fail). The kept words now join runs across a `/` or a dot as well as the marks (`WORDS_FORM` "2"), so a mask such as `TK-##/#` is found in them; words kept by 5.79 are read again once from the search index, not the files, at the first match. **Rebuild** remakes everything. Each Finder keeps what each kept word mentions (`Finder.in_run`), as the same tags recur across items. Items indexed together (a save, a tree file's or a template's pages) share one Finder and change the glossary's version once (`tagged.batch`), rather than reading the glossary again after each item that made a new label; and `search.rebuild` no longer finds tagged labels item by item, as `tagged.rebuild` then remakes them all.
- **Import and Export.** A row whose key is a suggested label's adds it to the glossary as the list's (imported, with its description). Export has the suggestions added to the glossary, and not those still only suggested.
- **Search** lists a suggested label first like any tagged label, "New, from the Instrument tag pattern · mentioned in 3 items".

**Measured** (made up, offscreen): the worst masks allowed over half a megabyte of text made to make them try hardest: under 0.5 s each, with the test suite running beside it (a capture's extracted text is at most 200,000 characters by default). One item indexed with two patterns: 1 ms. At an extreme size, 30,000 items' 347,000 kept words with 20,000 glossary labels and random 2-to-4-letter tags: a pattern change 5 s, making 90,000 suggestions (far more than a plant's would), and Try on everything 2.7 s. A real data root's are far fewer, and its tags recur.

**Not built:** descriptions and fuzzy matching (stage 32, set aside, decision 380). A key date's description is matched for a new label at the next matching (a pattern or glossary change, or Rebuild), as key dates are written apart from a task's other fields.

**Tests:** `tests/test_tagged_patterns.py` (new, in the fast run): the mask language (marks never required, spelling, optional parts, quotes, literals, `/`, a space in the mask, Allow a space, capitals), sixteen masks that can't be used and why, the examples, the worst masks over long texts timed, Never a tag, patterns from settings, a glossary label first and Never for patterns only, words joined across `/` and `.`, a pair a pattern finds as both tags (and only one where the mask can't spell the other), A/B/C as three, new labels on a saved capture with their chip, a pattern change from the kept words with reading any file made to fail, items indexed together changing the glossary's version once, a respelled or removed pattern, Never a tag removing suggestions, an old cache read again, a task's own fields, Add to glossary and Export, Same as with the owner's changes moved, Not a tag, a suggestion added by hand or imported, an alias taking a suggestion's name, the Suggested list's order and places, search, the tester, Try on everything changing nothing, settings.json, and docs.db's copy. `tests/test_tagged_patterns_ui.py` (new): the section's means and tester, Try on everything and Not a tag, Save settings, a bad mask or entry refused, the Suggested list and a suggestion's commands, Add to glossary through its dialog, Same as…, Not a tag with Undo, a capture's new chip and Patterns….

**Built in the `tagged-patterns` worktree** (2026-11-06, overnight), from `main` at `cd75434` (release 5.101, schema version 23), merged to `main` as `6ad27cf` and on GitHub 2026-11-07, without a hand test (the owner's choice). `__version__` stays 5.101, the highest; 5.80's changelog line sits between 5.81's and 5.79's. Its corporate test is the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-80-test.md`, where the owner tries their real conventions privately. The full run: 2896 passed, 2 skipped, and 1 failed, `test_wheel_window.py::test_an_arrow_runs_its_slot_as_it_comes_up` (the wheel window not visible yet, with other worktrees' runs going at the same time; nothing here touches the wheels). Its file passes on its own (52) and with the tests run before it (228). Ruff: no findings. With the pair rule (2026-11-07): 2,899 passed and 2 skipped, the wheel test among them; A/B/C's tagged tests after it, 95 passed.

## Decisions (release 5.80)

- Decisions 372 to 375 and 377 of the tagged labels plan, as the owner took them (recommended) and approved in the mock.
- **A pair a pattern finds is both tags,** the owner's choice on 2026-11-07 (the mock, and this release as first built, found only P-204A), when the mask allows the other letter; and **"P-204A/B/C" is all three,** for glossary labels too, the owner's choice the same day.
- **A mask is read in capitals:** `x` is `X`, any capital letter (the mock read a lower-case `x` as the letter X itself), and other letters are themselves in capitals. The text's letters must still be capitals.
- **A space typed in a mask may stand in the text** whether or not Space is ticked (the mock needed the tick, so `XX ###` spelled AB123 as "AB 123" but never found "AB 123").
- **A mask's limits:** at most 32 characters and 4 optional parts, so no mask can hang a save; a letter and a digit outside brackets (the mock counted optional ones, so `X[#]` would have matched every capital letter); no mark at its start or end, nor two in a row; and only `/` and `.` as other punctuation, the ones a tag holds.
- **The kept words join across `/` and `.`** (form "2"), so a mask with either is found in them, as decision 377 asks a pattern change to be.
- **Settings' section is a card in the Settings view,** saved with Save settings, rather than the mock's window with its own Save; the Tagged labels view's **Patterns…** opens it. **Never a tag** is one line, separated by commas, as Settings' event words are, rather than the mock's chips with ×.
- **Try on everything isn't run again on each key** (the mock did, over its few items): over a real data root it can take seconds, so the list says "The patterns changed. Try on everything again."; Not a tag from it runs it again.
- **Each pattern needs a name of its own,** as a new label says which pattern found it.
- **docs.db keeps a copy of the patterns** in `app_state`, made from settings.json at each start: a save is matched where only docs.db is at hand.
- **Not a tag forgets the owner's own changes on it** (a hand-added one would otherwise keep it), and its Undo puts back only the list.
- **A new chip** is the caution colours with "new" after it, as the detail pane's HTML can't draw the mock's dashed border.

## Release 5.122: Email conversations on the task page (2026-11-11)

The owner's ask of 2026-11-11: emails are a core source, so a section of their own on the task page, apart from files and links, showing the conversations of a task and its subtasks as one view, with a click opening the email chain. The design and its decisions are the design workspace's `docs/mods/email-section-plan.md` (decisions 455 to 463, built as recommended for the owner's word). Built from `main` at 5.118; 5.119 and 5.120 (rows and bullets) and 5.121 (search filters) are in their own worktrees. No schema change, no new package.

- **What makes a conversation** (decision 457; `docrepo/email_conversations.py`, no Qt): the emails with one conversation key (K8's GUID), with every email whose In-Reply-To or References names one of them, by union-find over every live capture's emails, once per model (`all_of`). Never the subject. The same email captured twice is listed once (`also` names its other captures, its tasks joined). An email capture with no `capture_emails` rows is one email (decision 463), its sender read from its From line. `CaptureEmail` gains `in_reply_to` and `refs`, read from the columns release 5.101 added.
- **The section** (455, 456, 458; `Pages.conversation_rows`, `task_parts["emails"]`, `task_header.GROUPS`): "Email conversations" after Subtasks, open until folded, not there without an email; over the page's With subtasks scope, newest first, the first five then "Show all n conversations" (`PageState.conversations`, `fold:conversations`). Each row: the mail mark, the subject in the text's colour, the count in grey, the subtask's chip, a grey line of who (`who`, the owner as "you") and the newest email's first words (`snippet` of `newest_words`), the newest day at the end. The head's command is New T-mail (`tmail:`).
- **A conversation's page** (459, 461, 462; `Pages.conversation_page`, `docrepo/ui/conversation_page.py`): selected as `("conversation", "<task>/<conversation>")`, drawn in the detail pane as a capture's page is; `exists` keeps it through a refresh, and `find` knows it by any of its keys or emails. Back to the task on its first line; the facts; each email oldest first, with To, its changed subject, "Newest", its own words (`email_text` finds its part of the capture's content text by the header lines `EmailItem.text` wrote, `own_words` cuts at the quoted emails) and Show the quoted emails (`more:q:`), the owner's words with it, its capture's files once, and Open in Outlook, Reply all and Open capture. The bar: Open in Outlook (primary) and Reply all, for the newest email; Reply all on the page is a T-mail of the page's task (`conversation_page.reply_task`).
- **Open in Outlook** (460): `outlook.show_email` finds the email by its EntryID and StoreID and calls Display, changing nothing; False (not found) or an OutlookError (Outlook not open) falls to the saved `.msg` through `opener.start`. The hard rules say so.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_email_conversations.py` (21, no Qt: one key, a reply with a new key joining, the subject never joining, an email captured twice, discarded captures and tasks, an email capture without rows, the scope newest first, own words at each kind of quote, a forward kept whole, each email of a two-email capture, snippets and people, the section and its Show all, the page in order with its links and the quoted emails unfolded, and `show_email` with a fake Outlook) and `tests/test_email_section.py` (6, offscreen: the section open on the project, the page and its bar through a refresh and back, Open in Outlook by place and by `.msg`, Reply all and New T-mail from the page, Show all).

## Release 5.121: Search everything's filters (2026-11-11)

The owner's ask, in the search training of 2026-11-11: File search's `type:`, `from:` and `after:` in Search everything too, keeping `#task`. Asked back, the owner chose Search everything only (Task search and Find in this view stay as they are) and to retire 5.108's place words (the design workspace's `docs/mods/search-filters-plan.md`, decisions 447 to 454). No schema change, no new package.

- **Reading it** (`search.read`, `_apply`, `_Reading`): File search's filters anywhere in what's typed, read with `finder`'s own patterns (`_OPERATOR`, `_DATE_OPERATOR`, `_EXTENSION`) and dates (`read_day`), so the two speak one language. A filter at the end needs no space after it when its value is whole. Then a `#task` at the start of what's left (`task_named`, as before), then the words. A `#` later on is a word ("work order #48213"). Any filter it can't read (`_ANY_FILTER`) is never a word to find: one still being typed at the end gets a hint (`HINTS`) and is left out; one that names nothing gets the hint and lists nothing. With no filter and no task, the scope is None and the whole of it is words, as before 5.108.
- **`type:`** (`type_place`, `_joined`): File search's file types (`finder.TYPE_WORDS`) and Search everything's kinds (`_KINDS`: file, link, folder, note, email, capture, task, person, label, tagged, copy), singular or plural; several add up.
- **What each keeps** (`_Keeper`, decision 451): `from:` an email capture by its emails' senders or its From line (the index's body, for an email from before 5.74), a document by its first capture's sender or by its source, a saved copy by its capture; the dates by a capture's saved day, a document's first capture's day, a saved copy's day (its name's, else its capture's), a task's created day, a notes page's last edit (`Model.page_edits`); `is:pinned` the pinned documents (`Model.pins`); `tag:` what mentions the tagged label (`tagged.uses_of`); `in:` a task as `#` names one. People, labels and tagged labels have no sender or day, so they drop out under those filters. `is:missing` says it's Find files' and File search's.
- **An email capture** is one with rows in `capture_emails` or, from before release 5.74, an Outlook source, for `type:email` and `type:notes` alike.
- **The limit:** up to 200 of each kind once a filter or a task is read; 40 while one is only being typed.
- **The browse window** (`ui/lists/documents.py`): the heading names the filters ("Search PDF files from norvane since 1 Sep in Vendor selection: curve"); each hint is a line above the results; a search that starts with "search " and finds nothing says the place words went (`search.OLD_WAY`); a `from:` that matches no sender says so. Each of those lines has its whole text as a tooltip, as a long one is cut at the list's edge. A saved copy's result shows its name without its day, which is the row's date, as in the Files section (decision 429). The search box's tooltip teaches the filters. The `#` list (`ui/search_scope.py`) opens for a `#` at the start or after filters.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_search.py` (5.108's three place tests rewritten for the filters, and two more: a filter being typed or naming nothing, and what dates, `from:`, `is:pinned` and `tag:` keep), `tests/test_search_scope.py` (the # list after `type:links`) and `tests/test_search_filters_ui.py` (3, offscreen: the heading, a hint, the old words).
- **Not in it:** quotes and `-word` in Search everything; filler words (decision 413, still open as 5.109); filters in Task search and Find in this view.

## Release 5.118: Undo after a Move or Put under (2026-11-10)

The backlog's item 2: the consolidation mock's toast after a move, with Undo, which release 5.98 left for a release of its own (the owner's yes of 2026-11-07). The design and its decisions are the design workspace's `docs/mods/undo-moves-plan.md` (decisions 435 to 439, made in building it, as the mock had them). Built from `main` at 5.114; 5.115 to 5.117 are the Today reminders', in their own worktree. No schema change, no new package.

- **What Undo covers** (decision 435): Move…, Put under…, Also put under… and Remove from under… on the Edit menu, wherever it is (the task page's split button, a task row's right-click menu, Find files' task rows), and a quick task's Put under… on its row. Not Edit task's Parents, Discard… (Bring back is its way back), Edit selected, tree and edit files, or filing a capture.
- **Exactly where it was** (436): a task's parents, and its place among each parent's subtasks, are the order its links in `task_parents` were made in. `tasks.parent_links` reads them (number and parent) before the move; `tasks.restore_parents` takes away what the move left and puts each link back with its own number, so the task is under the same parents, in the same order, in the same place. A number taken since (only the newest link's can be, as SQLite gives a new row the highest number plus one) puts the task last under that parent, with the links after it following, so the parents keep their order.
- **When it's offered and when it refuses** (437): `task_menus.move` runs the move through the browse window's `_do`, then `say_undo`, so Undo lasts ten seconds or until the next line (decision 363's); it runs once, from the status line or Find files' footer. `restore_parents` refuses, changing nothing, when the task's links aren't what the move left, or a parent is gone or under the task by now; the line says why.
- **The status log keeps both changes** (438): Undo is one more Parents line as "Browse tool"; the status line says "Set back: Installation is under Reactor impeller upgrade again. The status log keeps both changes."
- **A move says what it did** (439, `edits.moved_line`): "… moved from under A to B.", "… is under B now, out of Quick tasks.", "… is under B now.", "… is under B too.", "… is no longer under A." and "… is a top-level task now.". From Find files (`TaskView._tell_move`), its footer says it with Undo and its list is drawn again; `UndoButton.offer` takes the tip ("Put it back under the parents it had, where it was.").
- **`BrowseWindow.move_task`** hands to `task_menus.move`, as the rules ask of new code in the browse window; `_pick_parent` and `task_menus.run`, `edit_menu` and `add_row_menus` carry `after` and `tell`. `SPEC.md`'s Edit line says what a move says and what Undo does.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_undo_moves.py` (new, 8, no Qt): a task back second of four, several parents in their order with the place under each, every kind of move undone, a quick task back under Quick tasks and still on Today, a number taken since, the refusals (moved again, a parent under the task now) changing nothing, nothing to restore, and the pages written. `tests/test_consolidation.py` (5 more, offscreen): Remove from under… on a row's menu with the status line's Undo and both status log lines, Move… on the task page's Edit menu with Undo gone after the next line, a move from Find files with its footer's Undo and tip and Undo running once, and Undo refused after another move. `tests/test_edits.py`'s move test has the new words.

## Release 5.117: repeating reminders (2026-11-10)

The last of the three releases from the owner's list of 2026-11-10 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/today-reminders-plan.md`, decision 416). Schema version 24; no new package.

- **The rules** (`docrepo/repeats.py`, no Qt): `week:mon,thu` (every week on those weekdays), `month:15` and `month:last` (a day of the month, or its last day when it's shorter), `workday:3` and `workday:last` (a workday of the month, counted on Settings' workdays, or its last when it has fewer), and `days:5` (every n days, counted from the last time). `parse` checks one, `normal` keeps it in one form, `describe` says it in words ("Every month on its 3rd workday"), `next_after` and `first_from` find its times, and `following` the time after one that came, or none past its Until.
- **The task's fields** (schema 24: `tasks.reminder_repeat`, `tasks.reminder_until`; `Task`, `tasks.FIELDS`): logged as "Reminder repeats", in words, and "Reminder until". A repeat given with no reminder starts with its first time from today; clearing it clears its Until; an Until needs a repeat. It isn't Recurrence, which stays a routine task's, for Capacity.
- **Each time it comes round** (`edits.next_reminder`, `reminders_to_today`): the task goes on Today, as any reminder's does (release 5.116), and its reminder moves on to the next time after today, so a week away gives one item, not several. Later holds this time only; Clear takes this time, and the repeat goes on from the next ("Reminder repeats" in the status log). A closed task's waits.
- **Edit task** (`ui/repeat_field.RepeatField`, a module of its own): **Reminder repeats** beside Reminder under More options: Doesn't repeat, Every week on (a tick box a weekday), Every month on day (1 to 31, Last), Every month on workday (1 to 23, Last), Every n days; and **Reminder until**, shown with a repeat. Changing the rule puts Reminder at its first time from today, in the form, before it's saved. More options opens by itself when one is set. The task page's When says it as **Repeats**, and `Task.md` as **Reminder repeats**.
- **The upgrade:** `tests/fixtures/roots/v23.zip`, the made-up sample at schema 23, made by `main` at `b1eb46c` (5.107) with `init` and `load-sample`, and the two key dates the earlier fixtures have, so the upgrade to 24 is tested with data in it.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_repeats.py` (39, no Qt: each rule in words, one form, what isn't a rule, the next time for each kind with short months and months with fewer workdays, every n days, starting today, Until; a repeat starting from today, Until's rules, each time on Today and moving on, a missed week giving one item, Clear, Later, Until ending it and a closed task, and Task.md), `tests/test_today_view.py` (Edit task's field), and `tests/test_migrations.py` with the version 23 root.

## Release 5.116: reminders onto Today, Later, and the due list waiting for a reminder (2026-11-10)

The second of the three releases from the owner's list of 2026-11-10 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/today-reminders-plan.md`, decisions 415, 417 and 418). No schema change, no new package.

- **A reminder puts its task on Today** (decision 415; `edits.reminders_to_today`, `CaptureApp.reminders_to_today`): each minute once it's past `reminder_time`, after the notification, every open task whose reminder has come, today or before, goes on Today as an item added by "Reminder", and its reminder is cleared, "Put on Today" in its status log. A task already on Today just has its reminder cleared; a closed task's reminder waits. The row says "reminder" beside its project, in the Today view and the side panel. Today's Reminders heading and the panel's Reminders fold are gone (decision 237, and 238's Add and Clear); the sidebar's Reminders list stays, for what's still to come. Clicking a reminder's notification opens Today, on the task.
- **Later** (decision 417; `docrepo/later.py`, `edits.later` and `undo_later`, `TodayList.later_menu`, `do_later`, `_later_other`): **Later…** on a task's row on Today and on Due in the next 7 days, in the Today view, and **Later** on the side panel's task rows and due rows, open one menu: Tomorrow, 3 days, 1 week, 2 weeks and 1 month, each with its day, and Other…, which takes a number of workdays or a day in words, as Parse and populate reads dates. Tomorrow and 3 days count the workdays in Settings; the weeks and the month keep the weekday or day of the month, moved to the next workday. A pick sets the task's Reminder to that day ("Later" in its status log) and takes its Today item off Today, closed as Removed by "Later"; Undo, for ten seconds, puts back the reminder it had and the item. A note has no Later. The menu is shown through `today.show_menu`, a function of its own, since PySide6 doesn't let a test stand in for `QMenu.exec`.
- **The due list waits for a reminder** (decision 418; `views.waiting_for_reminder`): Due in the next 7 days, in the Today view and the side panel, leaves out a task whose reminder is still to come, and its key dates. The sidebar's Overdue and next 7 days list keeps them.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_later.py` (16, no Qt: the menu's days from a Thursday and a Friday, a month on from the 29th, Other…'s words, no workdays set, reminders onto Today and used up, a task already there, a closed task's reminder, Later and its reminder bringing it back, Undo, a day that isn't after today, the due list), `tests/test_browse_window.py` (reminders as Today items with no heading, Later's menu and its Undo, Later from a due row and Other…, and T000019 waiting for its reminder on the due list) and `tests/test_today_panel.py` (reminders as the panel's rows, Later from a row and its Undo, no Later on a note).

## Release 5.115: ticking to update, Undo and Back on Today, drops on the Today view (2026-11-10)

The first of three releases from the owner's list of 2026-11-10, taken as proposed (the design workspace's `docs/mods/today-reminders-plan.md`, decisions 415 to 421; this one has 419 to 421). Built as 5.110 to 5.112 and numbered 5.115 to 5.117 at the merge, after 5.113 and 5.114, which merged first (scoped search is 5.108). No schema change, no new package.

- **Ticking opens the Update** (decision 421; `lists/today.close_today_item`, moved from `BrowseWindow` into the Today mixin): Done on a task, from the Today view, the side panel's tick box, its Done or Space, opens the capture window as an Update on the task, its text starting "Done today: ", with no question. "Update the task too?" is gone, with `today_ask` and `answer_today`; Close the task is the capture window's State. Undo, for ten seconds, puts the item back and takes away the Update if nothing was typed in it (`CaptureWindow.drop_if_untouched`, as Discussed's Undo). A note, or a discarded task, is just done.
- **Undo in the panel, and Back on Today** (decision 420): `close_today_item` takes a `tell`, and the side panel passes its own line under the list, so Done and Remove say what they did there, with Undo, as a state change has since 5.76. Each row of Done today, in the Today view and the panel, has **Back on Today** (`put_back_today`, `edits.reopen_agenda_item`), for the rest of the day.
- **Drops on the Today view** (decision 419; `RowTree.take_file_drops`): a view can let files, folders, links and Outlook emails be dropped on its rows, read as the capture window reads a drop (`capture_window.read_drop`); `stop_row_moves`, at the start of every view, turns it off again. The Today view takes them on any task's row: Today's, Reminders', Due in the next 7 days' and Done today's. A drop opens the capture window as an Update on that task with it (`drop_on_task`, which the side panel now calls too). An email comes through Outlook's selection, as a drag the capture window's own drop target doesn't take does (release 5.33.1); a file only a program holds, such as an attachment dragged out of an email, is refused there with the capture window's words, and the side panel's cover takes it.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_today_view.py` (6: a file and a link dropped on rows, refused drops on a heading and a note, no drops in other views, Done's Update and its Undo, Back on Today), `tests/test_today_panel.py` (Done's Update and the panel's Undo, an Update kept when something was typed, Remove's Undo, a note just done, Back on Today, Space), and the Today tests in `test_browse_window.py` and `test_problems_undo.py` without the question.

## Release 5.114: files and search results, less crowded (2026-11-10)

The owner's ask: "fix how files and search results are displayed. They're too cluttered in almost every visual in which they appear." The review, with each surface before and after, is the design workspace's `docs/ui-design/file-rows.md` (decisions 427 to 434). No schema change, no new package.

- **One recipe** (`docrepo/file_rows.py`, no Qt): `type_icon`, `type_colour` and `type_name` give every list the same mark for a type; `doc_places` reads once where each document is (its tasks, and the day its first capture came in) and `place_words` says it ("Reactor impeller upgrade and 1 more", "Not filed yet"); `dated_name` splits a saved copy's day off its name; `without_title` takes a row's own title out of its snippet; `folder_of` gives a preview the folder; `GROUP_NAMES` the plural headings.
- **Snippets** (`text.snip`, used by `search.snippet` and `finder.snippet`): start and end at a word when one is near, leave out an email's addresses and its From:, To:, Cc:, Sent:, Date: and Subject: labels, and a stray full stop after the "…".
- **Search everything** (`ui/lists/documents.py`): a When column; the result count after the heading's title; group headings in the plural with how many follow; each result a `Line` with its type's mark in its colour (an email capture the mail mark), and one quiet line: where it is, then the matched words or its description (a task's: the tasks above it). The Classic look keeps its rows.
- **The Documents view** (`ui/lists/tasks.py`): columns Document and Added. A chip only for a status that isn't Current; the tooltip gives the type, the status and the day it was last checked.
- **The Files group and Files tab** (`detail.Pages.files_body`, `ui/page_view.py`'s `ft:` marks): a row a file, with the type's mark, the name as a link in the text's colour (without a saved copy's day), the subtask it's on, its description under it and the day it came in at the end; each file once (a saved copy stands for its original on the same task); the kind only for an old version or two files that share a name; "Show all n in Find files (O)" after the rows, or "Find files … to search, preview and open them (O)".
- **Find files and file search** (`ui/result_list.py`, `task_view_pages.where_html`, `ui/file_search.py`): a file's second line is its description, or where it came from when it has none; no "in its text:"; marks only after the task's name; the preview's Where is the folder, wrapping at backslashes, with the path as its tooltip; file search opens at 880 × 560 (720 × 520 before).
- **The capture window** (`CaptureWindow._add_file`, `_file_note`): a file's row is two lines, the type's mark (its kind the tooltip and accessible name) and the name, then the description with Save a copy and Extract information beside it; a file that stays where it is says nothing beside its name.
- **The notes page** (`NotesEdit._quiet_file_words`, `file_name_end`): a file line's "File:" and description in secondary text, its name in the link's blue. Only colours change; the page's words don't.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_file_rows.py` (9, no Qt: types, places, saved copies' days, folders, titles taken out of snippets, the headings, tidy snippets, the Files group's HTML) and `tests/test_file_rows_ui.py` (5, offscreen: the Documents view, search results, a capture's file row, a notes file line, the `ft:` mark); `tests/test_result_list.py` updated for the second line, and `tests/test_list_rows.py` for a saved copy in search with its file's mark (it had the task's state icon). The full run: 3,126 passed, 2 skipped; the two it flagged, a test fixed after it started and a wheel timing test slowed by two runs at once, pass on their own.

## Release 5.113: a calmer task page, and the notes' outline (2026-11-10)

The owner's ask of 2026-11-10: the task detail panel "is still cluttered and not visually appealing", to be made clearer, cleaner, better organized and more appealing; and the navigation panel for the notes' headings discussed before and never built (the notes workspace plan's decision 349, which put an outline at the editor's left, hidden on the task page until shown). The design and its decisions are the design workspace's `docs/mods/task-panel-plan.md` (decisions 422 to 426, built as recommended), with pictures before and after. Built from `main` at 5.108; 5.109 is kept for scoped search's decision 413; 5.110 to 5.112, kept for the Today reminders when this was built, went unused when they were numbered 5.115 to 5.117. No schema change, no new package.

- **What made it read as cluttered**, from pictures of 5.108 at the usual width: about a dozen different left edges (the title, the chips, the status text inside its card, the groups' chevrons, the subtask rows, the Notes frame and the history each started somewhere else); the closed groups run together on one line ("› When and who Agenda, 36 h › Files 5 Find files › Details"), so a summary read as the next group's title; three type sizes on the fact line; a status card whose tint the new look never drew (its style was only in the Classic sheet), so its words floated, indented, on white; a header band inside the Notes frame unlike every other section; and an open Details drawn in the middle of the pane.
- **The facts grid** (`Pages.facts_grid`, `status_value`, `labels_value`; decision 422): under the title and the description, Status and When's rows on the left, Who's rows and Labels on the right, each a grey name beside its value, both at the Body size (the grid's `rel` words become `relb`, Body size in the secondary colour); one column under 540 px; no wider than 760 px. Status is the state's mark and name with the state menu's chevron, then the priority, markers and Plant event chips; Meeting only when no date row already says it. Labels are names, inherited ones in secondary text. It takes the place of the chips, the fact line and the When and who group, whose methods (`fact_line`, `when_who_summary`) went. `page_head` takes the grid (`grid=`) and keeps its old shape for `task_page`.
- **The sections** (`task_header.py`; decision 423): Key dates, Agenda, Subtasks, Links, Files and Details each a row of its own, open or closed, in the page's order; no shared row. Every head is the same: the chevron hanging in the margin (`HANG`), the title, a count in the secondary colour (`<key>_short`), and the section's one command at the right (`detail.section_head`: New task under this one, Open agenda, Find files). The Files body leaves out its line about Find files (`files_body(intro=False)`), which is the head's command now. Details' body is grid rows (`grid_column`). The head has a trailing stretch, so an open section with no summary no longer centres.
- **One left edge** (decision 423): the new look's page margin is 24 px (`BrowseWindow`, 16 before), and the task page's HTML parts (`QTextBrowser#fitView`) have no padding or white of their own, so the title, the grid, the status card, the sections' titles, the Notes heading, the frame and History start on one line.
- **No rail** (decision 424): the grid holds what the rail held on a wide pane, so `task_parts` makes none and the sections are the same at any width. `task_page` (the whole page, for reading) keeps its rail.
- **The status card** (decision 425): a bar down its left side in the accent (grey with no status yet: `empty`), its tint in the new look at last, "Current status · Mon 5 Oct" in Caption, and "8 changes ›" at its right, which opens the status log.
- **The Notes heading** is a section's head over the frame (`notes_head`), not a band inside it; the frame is one line with rounded corners, its bottom edge the accent while typing (decision 130 kept).
- **The outline** (`docrepo/ui/notes_outline.py`, `TaskPane`'s outline methods; decision 426 and the notes workspace plan's 349): a column at the notes' left inside the frame, listing the page's headings and days (`NotesEdit.outline`), the owner's indented by level and a day in Caption without its year when the page's days share one, painted by its own rows (`_Rows`). It marks where you are: the heading over the caret while you type, else the last heading to reach the top of the view (at the page's end, the last in view); a heading picked in it stays marked until the page is scrolled by hand. A click goes there (`jump_to`). The pane has one scroll bar, so `OutlineSlot` keeps the column's place and moves the list down it as the page scrolls, under the sticky line; a list taller than the view scrolls on its own, and a short page's slot is at least as tall as its first eight rows. It's read again a moment after the page changes, and at once when a page is put in (`NotesEdit.page_set`, new), so it never shows the page before. Its button on the Notes heading (`outline` icon, new) shows or hides it for every task (`app_state` `notes_outline`; shown until hidden); Jump to's button shows only while the outline doesn't; where the notes would keep less than 360 px of text (`OUTLINE_MIN_TEXT`) it steps aside and its button goes.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_detail.py` (the page in parts: the grid, the sections' summaries and short words, no rail, one column when narrow, a meeting's chip), `tests/test_notes_edit.py` (the outline: its entries and words, the first marked, a pick goes there and stays marked, it follows the page shown, hidden and shown for every task, stepping aside), `tests/test_browse_window.py` (each section its own row, the counts) and `tests/test_pictures_tables.py` (the Notes heading over the frame).
- **Not in it:** the command bar (its wrap with Meeting notes in a narrow pane is as before); the Task view and Find files; the notes' own words (the day dividers still print their year).

## Release 5.108: Search everything in one place (2026-11-10)

The owner's ask: to type loosely in Search everything where to look, as a convention rather than a form: "search excel files" and words, "search links" and words, "search #Reactor impeller upgrade" and words (the design workspace's `docs/mods/scoped-search-plan.md`, decisions 409 to 414).

- **Reading it** (`search.read`): "search", then a place, a task after `#`, or both in either order, then the words. A `#` may start it on its own. It returns a `Scope` (its name for the heading, the groups it lists, the file types it keeps, whether its captures have an email, and its tasks) and the words. Small words around them ("in", "for", "my", "all") are passed over. With no place or task it knows, everything typed is words, so nothing typed before now searches differently.
- **The places:** a file type by `finder.TYPE_WORDS` (File search's own words), files, links, folders, notes, emails, captures, tasks, people, labels, tagged labels and saved copies; with or without "files" or "documents", singular or plural.
- **A task** (`search.task_named`): the longest whole title what follows the `#` starts with, as the # list writes it; or a task's ID; or, from the first word, every task with a word of its title starting that way. The scope is the task and every task under it.
- **The # list in the search box** (`docrepo/ui/search_scope.py`, `ScopeList`): the shared task list under the box while a `#` word is being typed, with closed and parked tasks; Enter, Tab or a click writes the task's name and a space; Esc hides it and leaves what's typed.
- **Searching** (`search.search`): the same search, with each group listed only when the scope has it, documents and saved copies kept by `overview.file_type`, a task's items kept by their task (a document by the captures that brought it and its saved copies), and up to 200 of each kind instead of 40. A scope with no words lists everything in it. Notes are notes pages and captures without an email; emails are captures with one, and .msg files.
- **The heading** says where it looked ("Search Excel files: pump curve", "Search Links in Reactor impeller upgrade"), and a search of everything says how to look in one place. The box's tooltip says it too.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_search.py` (reading the scope, each place listing only its own, a task's scope) and `tests/test_search_scope.py` (the # list in the box, offscreen).
- **Not built:** nothing drops filler words ("information", "forms"): every word after the scope must be in the item (decision 413, open).

## Release 5.107: consultants (2026-11-10)

The owner's ask (the design workspace's `docs/agents/brainstorm.md`, UC5, and `docs/mods/consultants-plan.md`, KB decisions 68 to 76): an agent made from the documents of one task and the tasks under it, to ask questions of them in VS Code. DocRepo makes everything but the map, and calls no LLM. Built from `main` at 5.105; release 5.106 is the Today panel's, in its own worktree.

- **A file's text by section** (`extract.sections_of`, `Section`): a Word file by its heading styles (a style based on one counts), with each table as a Markdown table in its place, a table inside a cell in brackets, text boxes read, and a table of more than 40 rows printed in parts with its header row again, each part a section. With no heading styles, a short numbered line ("3.2 Start-up") or one all in bold is a heading. A deck is a section a slide, with its notes; a PDF a section a page, headed by the bookmarks that start on it, read plainly or, with `--pdf-layout`, with its columns kept in place. Text, CSV, Excel and `.eml` files are one section. Nothing is cut at the text limit. `_pptx` now reads through `_pptx_slides`, with the same text as before.
- **The consultant's folder** (`consultants.build`): `Consultants\<name>` in the data root, with `corpus\<document ID>.md` (each heading with its reference, `D000123#s4`, `#slide-7` or `#page-12`; a line of the document that starts with `#` escaped), `Index.md` (every document, how it was read, the ones to check and each one's outline), `manifest.json` (each file's size, date and SHA-256, and its sections), `.github\copilot-instructions.md` and the two agents in `.github\agents` (the consultant with read and search, its trainer with edit too), and `<name>.code-workspace` (the folder read-only, and Copilot's folder beside it when Settings names one). The rules and the agents are fixed templates, filled with the name, the task's title on one line, and counts.
- **What it reads** (`scope_files`, `read_document`): saved copies, tracked originals and email attachments of the task and every task under it that isn't discarded, each document once, from the file itself. A file that's out of reach or can't be read falls back to its `extracted.txt` as one section, and says so; a PDF with pages that have no text is "partial". One bad file never stops the rest.
- **Made again** (`build`): the same name and task rebuilds it. A document that left the scope keeps its corpus file, with one line saying it left (DocRepo deletes nothing), and the manifest lists it under `gone`. The same name for another task is refused.
- **The map** (`check_map`, `check_page`, `keep_map`): the trainer writes `<name>-catalogue.md`, `-vocabulary.md`, `-topics.md` and `-exam.md` into Copilot's folder, which DocRepo only reads. Each is checked: a catalogue entry names a document of the consultant; a vocabulary line has a word that's in the corpus; every reference in the topics and the exam is a section in the manifest; and no page holds a web address, a path, a code block or a heading that isn't an entry. `--keep` writes each page with no problems to `map\<page>.md` under a line naming the build it was checked against.
- **What changed since** (`status_lines`): files added, changed, gone from the task or out of reach, by SHA-256, and a map page that's missing or of an earlier build. Exit code 2 when the documents changed.
- **`SafeFS.write_text`** also writes `.code-workspace` files, which are JSON.
- **The rules file** gains the consultants' paragraph. `GLOSSARY.md` gains **Consultant**, **Corpus**, **Map** and **Trainer**; `SPEC.md` gains section 14 and the three commands.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_consultants.py` (34: sections of each kind, the folder's files, the templates and a task's title kept on its line, names and tasks checked, the fallback, what changed and a rebuild, a good map kept, 21 bad pages refused, a missing page and an earlier build, and the commands).
- **Not in it:** a command in the browse tool and a flag there for a consultant whose documents changed; section numbers that Word makes by style (the corpus shows the heading's words); running the exam.

## Release 5.106: the Today panel's width and drops, and windows restored (2026-11-10)

The owner's two voice memos of 2026-11-09 and 10, read back and answered "Yes, all three" the same day (the design workspace's `docs/mods/today-panel-plan.md`, decisions 405 to 408). No schema change, no new package, no mock.

- **Windows restored** (decision 405; `docrepo/ui/front.py`): `bring_to_front` takes a window's minimized state off (keeping Maximized) before `show()`, `raise_()` and `activateWindow()`. Qt's `show()` keeps a minimized window minimized, so the browse tool's hotkey only flashed its taskbar button. Used where the browse tool (`show_browse`, and Find files' four links to it), a Find files window (`open_task_view`), file search (`open_it`) and the capture window (`show_capture`, `_come_forward`) come forward. The frameless windows can't be minimized and are as they were.
- **The width at rest** (decision 406; `panel_rules.dragged_width`, `rest_width`, `REST_WIDTH_KEY`; `today_panel._Grip`): a grip on the inner edge, shown only at rest, sets the width at rest from `MIN_REST_WIDTH` (100) to `hover_width`, live as it's dragged (`place_window(animate=False)`), kept in `app_state` (`today_panel_rest_width`) on release; `rest_width` in the wheels config is the width until one is dragged, and **Reset the width at rest** on the top line's menu goes back to it. The pointer on the grip counts as off the panel (`on_grip`), so it doesn't widen there. The count at the top no longer holds the panel at its text's width.
- **Drops on a task** (decision 407; `TodayPanel.drop_targets`, `target_at`, the drag events, `drop_on`): the panel takes Qt's drops of files, folders and links (`capture_window.read_drop`) on Today's task rows and on the tasks under Reminders and Due in the next 7 days (`_add_line` keeps each line's task). A drag over it widens it, closes a task page to show the list, holds it wide and marks the row under the pointer (`tpRow[drop="true"]`); elsewhere the drag is refused. A drop opens the capture window as an Update on the task (`BrowseWindow.open_capture`, so a held capture goes to Drafts first) and hands it the drop (`CaptureWindow.dropped`).
- **Emails and files a program holds** (decision 408; `today_panel._DropCover`, `drop_target.DropTarget`'s `allowed`): DocRepo's own drop target as a cover over the panel, drawing its tasks where their rows are, the one under the pointer filled, with "Drop on a task: …" on its top line. `DropTarget` takes an optional `allowed` question: off a task the pointer says no, and a drop there returns no effect without reading anything. What it took goes to `CaptureWindow.take_drop`, as from the capture window's own cover.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_front.py` (8: the restored state, a minimized and a maximized window, a hidden one left minimized, the browse tool's hotkey, Find files opened again, its Task page link, a capture hotkey), `tests/test_today_panel.py` (15 more: the width rules, the grip's place and when it shows, dragging and keeping and resetting, the pointer on the grip, the config's width, the drop targets, a file and a link dropped, the marked row, refused drops, a task page closed by a drag, a due task, the cover's question and its drop) and `tests/test_email_drop.py` (2: a drop target that takes drops only where it's told, and one whose question fails).

## Release 5.105: T-mails, and each email on its own (2026-11-10)

The knowledge base's stage K8 (the design workspace's `docs/knowledge-base/email-conversations-plan.md`, KB decisions 32 to 46): release A's fixes from the corporate computer's first results (2026-11-09, on 5.103), and release B, which the owner asked to have in the same release ("Can we include Release B in 5.105?", then "Yes, build it", 2026-11-10). Release C, a T-mail captured when it's sent, stays a release of its own: it knows a sent copy by the index B gives it, which this release's corporate test confirms first.

**What the corporate computer showed.** The fill worked: the pinned window and the capture window filled the picker "from conversation", and a hand pick and `#` won. Two emails from two tasks didn't empty it, and a reply whose subject was changed lost the task, since Exchange gives such a reply a new conversation index. A new email had no index when made (check C1, so KB decision 39 applies); a reply had one at once, carrying the original's; making emails and replies met no guard (C3); a reply from a saved `.msg` kept the conversation (C4). Outlook gave no Message-ID, In-Reply-To or References itself; the `.msg` copy had the Message-ID.

- **Each email on its own** (`conversations.email_filing`, `pick_emails`, `message_ids`): an email already captured counts with its own capture's task, found by its Message-ID (the latest live capture), before its conversation's latest filing; one that isn't follows its conversation's latest filing, as in 5.101; a reply whose conversation isn't known follows the captured email its In-Reply-To names, else the newest its References names. The two-task case: two captured emails of one conversation, filed to two tasks, both followed the conversation's latest filing, so they agreed; now they disagree, and the picker says "None: these emails belong to …". The tooltip says which way the task came ("This email was filed to …", "The email this one answers was filed to …"); `Filing.how` holds it. The capture window and the pinned window use `pick_emails`; `pick` stays for a list of keys. The captured emails, by Message-ID, are worked out once per model beside the conversations (`_known`).
- **New T-mail** (`outlook.new_t_mail`, decisions 35, 36 and 39): `CreateItem`; its index read at once; when none, `Save` (to Drafts, as AutoSave would) and read again; when still none, `give_index` writes one with `PropertyAccessor.SetProperty` (`conversations.new_index`: 0x01, the FILETIME's next 5 bytes, a new GUID) and sets the tracking flag; then `Display`. `Opened` says which ("made", "saved", "written", or "none" when nothing worked, which is still opened, and said). On the task page's bar it's a split button after Meeting notes (`page_commands._task_actions`), with **Reply all to the latest email** on its chevron (`t_mails.latest_menu`, `conversations.latest_email`: the newest email captured on the task, by when it was sent, with a place or a `.msg`), greyed with a tooltip when there's none. No key letter.
- **Reply all** (`outlook.reply_all`, decisions 37 and 38): `GetItemFromID` with the EntryID and StoreID, else `OpenSharedItem` on the saved `.msg` (closed with `olDiscard` after, so the file is unchanged), then `ReplyAll` and `Display`. On Find files' email rows (`task_view._actions_for`, the capture's first email) and beside each email in the capture page's Emails row (`replyall:` links, `detail.capture_page`).
- **Kept** (`conversations.record_t_mail`, `reply_task`; `docrepo/ui/t_mails.py`): a `t_mails` row with its task, kind, the email replied to, when, its key and whole index, so the conversation follows the task from then on. A reply is kept on the task it was clicked from when its capture is on it, else the capture's first task; an unfiled capture's reply is opened and not kept; a T-mail with no index isn't kept, and the status line says its replies won't find the task by themselves. The log line names the T-mail's ID, task and the key's first 8 characters.
- **The report** (`diagnose.Step.info` and `line`, `copy_words`, `_written_step`): the tracking flag "not set", the three headers "none through Outlook" and a new email's missing index are findings, shown without "ok" or "FAILED"; the copy's line adds In-Reply-To and References, yes or no, so the next corporate report says whether the changed-subject route has what it needs; **Try a new email with an index DocRepo writes** writes one as New T-mail would, reads it back and closes without saving.
- **The rules file** gains release B's sentence (DocRepo may save a new email to Drafts or give it a conversation index, and never sends one), and the try-outs' sentence its new try-out. `GLOSSARY.md` gains **T-mail**.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_t_mails.py` (26: the new index, Message-IDs, the fill's three ways and their order, T-mails kept or not, the latest email, a reply's task, New T-mail's three routes and its failures, Reply all's two routes and its failure, the report's lines and try-out, and no `.Send(` in the Outlook code) and `tests/test_t_mail_buttons.py` (9, offscreen: two emails in one drop from two tasks and from one, a changed subject in the window, New T-mail on the bar and its arrow, Find files' and the capture page's Reply all, an unfiled capture's reply). `test_conversations.py`'s try-out test and `test_browse_window.py`'s bar labels follow the changes.

**Not done here.** Release C (the Sent Items watch, the sweep and the capture made with no window) is next, after this release's corporate test. An email dragged in while "Mark filed emails in Outlook" is off still has no place kept, so its Reply all takes the `.msg` route, and Outlook doesn't mark the original as replied to; keeping the place on every drag would ask Outlook something on every drag, so it waits for the owner's word.

## Release 5.104: a Duration with only a Deadline gets Plan mode's grip (2026-11-08)

Release 5.48's one thing not built, put to the owner in its corporate test and answered yes on 2026-11-07: a task with a Duration and a Deadline but no Start or Due had no bar and no ⋮⋮ in Plan mode, only its deadline's diamond, so it couldn't be dragged onto the chart. No new date kind, no new way to set one (the rule of release 5.100): Plan mode's drop, which an undated task's name already has, reaches one more kind of row. No schema change, no new package, no mock.

- **Which tasks** (`timeline.is_unplaced`): a one-time task with a Duration and no Start or Due. `schedule.windows` gives it no window (the templates design's Dates: its hours aren't spread from the day it was made), so with a Deadline or key dates in view its row is those marks alone. A task with key dates and no Duration is as it was (release 5.31's choice): its name has no grip.
- **The row** (`Row.unplaced`, `Row.grip`): `unplaced` is set when such a task's own marks are in view; `grip` is `undated or unplaced`, and is what the chart's painting and hit test ask for the ⋮⋮. `Row.undated` and `Timeline.undated` keep their meaning (no dates at all), so the list under the chart and its fold are unchanged: a task with a Deadline isn't "with no dates".
- **The hint** (`_paint_grip_hint`): the dashed "Drag the name onto the chart to give it its 3 workdays" still runs across the chart for an undated task; beside a deadline or key dates it goes in the wider of the room to their left and to their right, and isn't drawn when the words don't fit there. The tooltip has them in any case.
- **The drop:** as an undated task's (`Drag` mode "new", `timeline.dropped` through `tasks.worked_out` with `dragged`): the day let go on, or the next workday, and its own Duration from there. The Deadline is never touched. `timeline.drop_said` ends with "past its Deadline Fri 20 Nov" when the Due lands after it, for any mode, as Edit task's small warning does; a what-if's drop says it too.
- **The words:** the tooltip, the line under the chart and the Classic look's card say "No Start or Due yet" for such a task, with its Deadline after it, where they said a Start DocRepo had made up from the day it was created.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_timeline.py` (the row, the tooltip, the drops' dates and words, and the key-dates-only row still without a grip) and `tests/test_timeline_drags.py` (the grip hit and dragged offscreen, the save, the bar then moved past its Deadline).

## Release 5.103: every task picker works as the `#` list does (2026-11-06)

The design workspace's task pickers plan (`docs/mods/task-pickers-plan.md`), item 117, stage 35, decisions 398 to 404, each a routine call the owner can change. The owner asked in chat on 2026-11-06: "I like the dropdown list navigation system we set for workflows where a task is selected using the # operator. Can you copy that system to all the other locations where navigating tasks with a dropdown selector is involved? (for example, any task search windows)". Built without a mock, as it copies a design already approved (releases 5.74 and 5.75). No schema change and no new package. 5.102 is another worktree's.

- **One task list** (`docrepo/ui/task_list.py`, decision 398). The `#` list's rows, levels and keys moved out of `items_panel.py` into `TaskList` and `TaskRowDelegate`, as they were: the 8 px slot with a top-level task's dot and Semibold name or another task's ring, "3 ›" with the chevron's backplate, a level's header row with its back chevron and its subtasks indented 16 px, and Up, Down, Right, Left, Enter and Tab (`handle_key`). Two things are new, for the pickers:
  - a row that can't be picked (`Words`: a section's heading, or a line such as "No open or parked task matches."), which Up and Down pass over and a click ignores;
  - in a list of tasks, an entry that isn't a task (the picker's None) keeps the slot empty, so the names line up.

  `task_colour`, `task_mark` and `subtask_options` moved there too. `RefPopup` (the `@` and `#` list) is now `TaskList` plus where it shows, at the text cursor, and Esc. Its old names (`_OPTION`, `_HEAD`, `REF_*`, `task_colour`) can still be imported from `items_panel`.
- **One matcher** (`items.task_matches`, `items.ancestor_titles`, decision 398): the `#` list's matching, ranking and path. `items.options` calls it for `#`, and every picker's search uses it. A search box strips what's typed, so a space after a whole name doesn't empty its list, as it closes the `#` list (decision 401).
- **The task picker** (`docrepo/ui/task_picker.py`, decisions 399, 400 and 404): a popup (a frame with Qt's Popup flag, as the Timeline's Display popover is) with the search box over a `TaskList`.
  - **With nothing typed:** Recent (each with its path), the None entry, then Tasks (the open top-level tasks), under grey Caption headings. Right goes into a task's open subtasks (Not started or In progress), and Left comes back; Right and Left move the box's cursor when the list can't use them.
  - **Typing** lists up to 12 matches with their paths, parked ones marked "(parked)" and, with `closed_too`, closed ones "(closed)". When nothing matches, a line says so, or Search everything is offered.
  - **Keys:** Enter or Tab picks the current row (the first, until you move), a click picks a row, and Esc closes.
  - **Size:** the caller's minimum width or its widest row, up to 560 px, and 14 rows before it scrolls.
  - **Gone:** phase 3's cascading menus, and the ↻ after a recurring task's name.
  - **Its API:** the constructor, `picked`, `aboutToShow`, `aboutToHide`, `popup`, `pick`, `closed_too`, `hint` and `no_match` are as they were, so no caller changed. New: `keep` (a test of a task's ID that leaves it out everywhere in the list), `modifiers` (the keys held with Enter) and `rows()`.

  It's every place `TaskPicker` was: the capture window's task picker and the pinned window's; the browse tool's `_pick_task` (the task page's Move from under…, Put under a task… and Also put under…, File to task, the Documents view's Task filter, Edit task's and bulk edit's Put under, a tie's task, Make tasks and Apply template); and Find files' Ctrl+O.
- **Task search** (`docrepo/ui/task_search.py`, decision 400): the picker as before, with its hint ("Enter opens its page; Ctrl+Enter its Find files") as a line under the box, closed tasks marked, no None, and Search everything on Enter when nothing matches. Ctrl+Enter is read from the key itself (`picker.modifiers`) as well as from the keyboard's state.
