## Release 5.102: the review's smaller items (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, section 4 (the design workspace's `docs/docrepo-review-2026-11-06-actions.md`): the smaller items, which weren't reviewed one by one; its items 1 to 14 were 5.87 to 5.96. 5.97 to 5.100 are the consolidation plan's. No schema change and no new package.

- **The dump** (`docrepo/dump.py`, `python -m docrepo dump`): every table of docs.db as JSON in `System\export\<date>` in the data root, a `<table>.json` each (the table's name, its columns, and its rows as objects of column and value) and `_dump.json` (which DocRepo, when, the schema version and each table's rows). So everything that lives only in SQLite (templates and their ties, task ties, pins, document uses, notes page versions, label keywords and parents, reference fields) has a plain-file way out. It reads docs.db through `db.connect_read_only` in one transaction, so the tables agree and nothing is changed or upgraded, and writes through SafeFS. A second dump on one day replaces the first's files, as `check`'s list does. The hard rules' list of what DocRepo writes names it.
- **A save made during a backup waits for it** (`db.BUSY_TIMEOUT`): every connection to docs.db waits up to 30 seconds for another to let go (SQLite's busy timeout, through `sqlite3.connect`'s `timeout`) where Python's default gave up after 5, so a save made while a backup copies a large database waits for the copy instead of failing with "database is locked". The backup's copy, the troubleshooting report and the dump open docs.db read-only through one function, `db.connect_read_only`, with the same timeout. The journal stays SQLite's default: WAL would let the save go on at once, but its `-shm` file isn't safe when the data root sits in a synced or network folder (a question for the owner, below).
- **One HTML escape** (`markup.esc`, `html.escape` with quotes): `detail.py`, the import dialog, the tagged labels' import dialog, the capture window (whose own didn't escape quotes), the items panel and Settings all use it; `detail.esc` stays as the name the other modules import. Parse and populate's, which escapes for a regular expression, is `parse.re_esc`.
- **Names in built SQL** (`db.check_names`): a helper that writes a table's or a column's name into a statement first checks it against its own allow-list, and refuses any other with ValueError: an agenda item's table (`agenda._exists`), a person's aliases and a label's keywords (`people._set_list`), a task's columns on create and edit (`tasks`, against `model.TASK_COLUMNS`, which a test keeps the same as the schema), and a tie's source (`tasks._end_ties`). The other f-string statements interpolate only `?` marks or a module's own constant list of names (`captures`, `conversations`, `templates`, `model`, `sample`, `search`, `overview`, `pins`, `ties`, `save`, and `people.update_person`'s two columns), so the constant is their allow-list.
- **Tests for modules no test imported**: the Task view's pages, its painted result list, Capacity's strip on the Timeline page, and `msg.py` over a forwarded email built by `tests/msg_builder.py`, which now writes an attached email as an embedded message.
- **Dead members gone** (each grepped through `docrepo/` and `tests/`, getattr strings and Qt slot names included): `task_view_pages.no_files` and `kind_label`, `CaptureWindow.hide_capture`, `RefPopup._current` (in `ui/items_panel.py`), `PeoplePicker.set_shares` and `wheel.Resolver.reset`. `items.item_text` stays: `tests/test_quick_input.py` uses it.
- **A size scale for new code** (`tokens.SIZE`, beside `SPACE`): row heights (tree, a list's heading, one-line, two-line), icon sizes (10 to 20) and control heights (24, 28, 32), as the code uses them. The literals already in `ui/` stay.
- **Upgrading a dependency** (`SPEC.md` section 2): a second venv, the full suite and `tests/test_real_look.py`, a look at the app, and one corporate scan, before any pin moves, PySide6's above all.
- **Docstrings** for the public functions of the files this release touches, some 130, except `ui/task_forms.py` and `ui/capture_window.py`, which the consolidation build is changing tonight, and Qt's own event handlers.

**Tests:** `tests/test_dump.py` (new): every table with its rows, docs.db's bytes unchanged, a second dump on one day, the write refused outside SafeFS's folders, one transaction, the command on a database a schema behind (not upgraded), and a data root not set up. `tests/test_db.py`: the busy timeout and the rollback journal, a save made while a reader holds docs.db waiting and then succeeding, the read-only connection, `check_names`, `TASK_COLUMNS` against the schema, and the helpers refusing other names. `tests/test_msg.py` (new, in the fast run): a forward with two files and the forwarded email attached as an item (left out, with its own file), its identifiers from its transport headers, the forwarded email read on its own, what isn't a `.msg`, times and headers. `tests/test_task_view_pages.py` (new): the header, crumbs, each kind's preview, the matched lines, the empty states and the count line. `tests/test_result_list.py` and `tests/test_capacity_strip.py` (new, offscreen): rows, keys, clicks and painting. Each hides and deletes its widgets (and the strip's tooltip) after a test, as the wheel's tests do: closed but kept, they made the wheel window lose the foreground in `test_wheel_window.py`'s first test, twenty minutes later in a full run. `tests/test_look.py`: the size scale.

**Built in the `small-items` worktree** (2026-11-06, overnight), from `main` at `cd75434` (5.101), merged to `main` as `4513843` and on GitHub 2026-11-07, without a hand test (the owner's choice). The full suite: 2878 passed, 2 skipped; ruff: no findings. Its corporate test is the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-102-test.md`.

## Decisions (release 5.102)

Routine calls, made while building:

- **No WAL.** The data root may be in a synced or network folder, where WAL's shared-memory file isn't safe, so docs.db keeps the rollback journal and a save waits for a backup's copy. Whether to move to WAL when the data root is known to be on a local drive is the owner's question.
- **A second dump on one day replaces the first**, as a second `check` does; the dump is a copy made for reading, never read back.
- **`items.item_text` kept**: the review counted it dead, but a test uses it.
- **Templates' statements unchecked**: they name only the module's own `_COLUMNS`, so a check would compare the list with itself.

## Release 5.48: Plan mode by workdays, the Timeline's hours with Involvement, and ties on the chart (2026-11-07)

Round 5's thread A, what the task templates design left until thread D's timeline work merged (the design workspace's `docs/mods/task-templates-design.md`: its Plan mode paragraph and the 5.48 row; items 64 to 66, decisions 189 to 191 and 196), and decision 259, the ties' lines and live preview, which decision 263 put in this release. Release 5.46 brought Plan mode's rules for a drop a release early (`set_timeline_dates` marks a drop as a drag), and what-ifs' drags (5.64) go through the same `tasks.update_task(dragged=True)`, so what was left is the bar while it's dragged, what the drop says, the hours text, and the ties on the chart. No schema change and no new package.

- **The hours text counts Involvement** (`timeline.hours`, `hours_words`): a bar's total, its hours a week, Colour bars by hours a week, the tooltip and the line under the chart count each person's share through `capacity.roles_of`, as Capacity does. On 12 h in all with Tom Becker at 25% and Mike Ortiz at 50%, the bar says 21 h, not 36, and the words give each person's own: "Priya Shah 12 h, Tom Becker 3 h (25%) and Mike Ortiz 6 h (50%) as assignees". A role with everyone at 100% reads as before. A task that runs through weekends counts its hours a week over its calendar days, seven a week, as Capacity spreads them.
- **A bar with a Duration keeps its workdays while it's dragged** (`timeline.dropped` and `drop_dates`; `TimelineChart._drag_to` and `_as_dropped`): the bar is drawn with the dates its drop will be saved with, worked out by `tasks.worked_out`, the save's own rules. Moved across a weekend it's drawn wider, Thu 5 Nov to Wed 11 Nov for five workdays, and isn't more work; with Runs through weekends it counts every day. An end stretched follows the pointer, as the new length. The tooltip says the dates, their workdays (or days), and what else the drop changes: "Mon 2 Nov → Wed 4 Nov · 3 workdays · Duration 5 → 3 workdays · assignee hours 30 → 18".
- **An undated task's name takes its own Duration** while it's dragged and when it's dropped, from the day it's let go on, or the next workday in Settings; with none, a working week as before. Its dashed track, its tooltip and the mouse's hint say which: "give it its 3 workdays", "give it a week". Plan mode's hint says "for its Duration, or a working week".
- **The drop says what changed** (`timeline.drop_said`, `drop_changes`): "Trial execution window: Due moved to Wed 4 Nov, Duration 5 → 3 workdays, assignee hours 30 → 18. Ctrl+Z undoes it.", "now Thu 5 Nov to Wed 11 Nov, still 5 workdays", or "Start and Due set: Mon 9 Nov to Wed 11 Nov, its own 3 workdays". A total stays only with h in all (decision 137, narrowed as the design says). In a what-if the drop says the same, and Review and apply's line adds the Duration and totals it changes (`whatif.lines`), beside "hours kept".
- **Settings' workdays reach the chart** (`TimelineView.workdays`, which the browse window sets): a Duration counts them while it's dragged, as the save does. They were Monday to Friday there.
- **A selected bar's lines** (decision 259; `timeline.tie_links`, `TimelineChart.tie_lines` and `_paint_ties`): thin lines in the accent from where each tie counts from (a dot on the source's start or end, or on its key date's strip) down or up to the tied task's row and along it, with an arrow into the Start or Due it sets: to what the bar is tied to, and from it, or its key dates, to what's tied to them. Nothing selected, or a bar with no ties, draws nothing. A line whose other end is folded away or filtered out isn't drawn. The mock didn't draw them, so their look is this release's.
- **What follows a dragged bar, shown where it will go** (`timeline.follows`, `TimelineChart._follow`): while a bar, a key date or a what-if's whole parent is dragged, each task tied to it, near or far, is a dashed bar at the dates the drop will give it, its own bar faded, the lines going with it, and the tooltip says "2 tasks tied to it move with it". The dates come from the model by `tasks.follow`'s own rules (`ties.placement`, then `tasks.worked_out`, each after what it's tied to; closed and discarded tasks stay; a task's key dates don't follow its dates), worked out again only when the drag's days change. A move that reaches another tree still asks on the drop (decision 249); the preview shows them all moving.
- `SPEC.md` (6.13, 6.14, 6.15 and the Timeline row in 8.3) and `README.md`'s Timeline line follow. `GLOSSARY.md` has no new term.

**Tests:** `tests/test_timeline_ties.py` (new, with no Qt): a chain of two ties moved and stretched, a Due tied before a Start, a closed task in the chain, and a key date's move, each previewed by `timeline.follows` and then saved, the two agreeing; a bar's ties both ways; and where a tie counts from. `tests/test_timeline_hours.py` (new, with no Qt): the design's three people and their total against `work_calendar` and the week's cells, the words, everyone at 100%, someone at 0% and everyone at 0%, Colour bars by hours a week, Runs through weekends' week, a routine task's each time, and each drop (moved, through weekends, stretched with h a day, h in all and h a week, a rate with plain dates, an undated task with and without a Duration, a bar ending at its deadline, a four-day week) worked out by the view and then saved, the two agreeing, with its words; and a what-if's Review and apply lines. `tests/test_timeline_drags.py` (new, offscreen): the bar's dates, its width and the tooltip while it's dragged and after, Ctrl+Z, through weekends, a stretched end's message, a name with and without a Duration, Settings' four-day week, a what-if's drop changing no task, a selected bar's lines (none without ties, both ways, a key date's on its strip, none to a folded row), and what follows a dragged bar, in Plan mode and in a what-if, matching what's saved.

**Built in the `timeline-hours` worktree** (overnight, 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-07), from `main` at `cd75434` (5.101), decision 259 added the same night, as decision 263 has it. Merged to `main` as `e4ddc4e` and on GitHub 2026-11-07, without a hand test (the owner's choice); its corporate test is the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-48-test.md`.

## Decisions (release 5.48)

- Decisions 189 to 191, 196 and 259, and the design's Plan mode paragraph, as the design has them.
- **Judgement calls, for the owner's hand test:**
  - **What a drop says is worked out before it's saved,** by the save's own `tasks.worked_out`, so the drag, the what-if and the message say the same; a test saves each case and checks the two agree.
  - **A name with a Duration let go on a day that isn't a workday starts on the next one,** as a working week's Saturday moves to Monday; with Runs through weekends it starts on that day. The design doesn't say. A typed Start on a Saturday still stays as typed.
  - **The tooltip while dragging says the drop's changes too,** so a rate's new total is seen before letting go.
  - **Hours a week count every day for a task that runs through weekends:** a five-day Thu to Mon task at 6 h a day read 50 h a week, where Capacity has 42. Hours a week otherwise still count Monday to Friday, as since release 5.29: `timeline.py` takes no settings, and Capacity's cells are what count.
  - **Each person's own hours read "Tom Becker 3 h (25%)",** as the task page's "3 h · 25%".
  - **Review and apply's line says the Duration and totals** a what-if's drag changes. The design only speaks of the drop's message.
  - **The lines' look** (decision 259 wasn't mocked): 1.2 px in the accent, a dot where a tie counts from, an elbow down or up to the tied task's row, and an arrow into its date. A tie counting from a key date starts on the key dates' strip.
  - **A line to a row that isn't on the chart isn't drawn,** rather than pointing at its folded parent: opening the parent shows it.
  - **The preview shows every task that would move,** even those in other trees, which the drop still asks about (decision 249).
- **Not built:**
  - **A task with a Duration and a Deadline but no Start or Due** has no bar and no grip in Plan mode, only its deadline's diamond: it isn't "undated", having a Deadline, and `schedule.windows` doesn't place it (5.46). So it can't be dropped on the chart. Whether it should get a grip, and be listed with the tasks that have no dates, is the owner's call.

## Release 5.101: emails that find their task (2026-11-06)

The knowledge base's stage K8, release A (the design workspace's `docs/knowledge-base/email-conversations-plan.md`, KB decisions 32 to 46, taken 2026-11-06; 35 the owner's). The owner asked for an email from a conversation that's already captured to arrive with its task filled in, and for a button on a task that opens an Outlook email whose replies know their task (a T-mail, K8's release B), captured on its task when it's sent (release C). Release A is the part that needs no new Outlook action: the identifiers, the rule, the backfill and the checks. 5.97 to 5.100 are the consolidation plan's. Schema version 23 (5.79 took 22); no new package.

- **Outlook's identifiers, kept with each email** (`docrepo/msg.py`, `outlook.item_identifiers`, `capture_emails`): the conversation index, ConversationID, topic and tracking flag, and the Message-ID, In-Reply-To and References, read from the `.msg` without Outlook (from their own properties, else from the transport headers), or field by field through Outlook when the email couldn't be copied whole. Each email's row holds them, with its conversation's key, and capture.md's `emails` list names each one's conversation and Message-ID. Release 5.93's places (EntryID and StoreID) move from `app_state` into the same rows.
- **A conversation follows its task** (`docrepo/conversations.py`, KB decisions 32 and 33): the key is the GUID in the conversation index's header (MS-OXOMSG 2.2.1.3), never Outlook's ConversationID, which can be a hash of the subject, and never the subject. A conversation follows the task it was last filed to, by a live capture or by a T-mail, a discarded task skipped. In the capture window and the pinned window, an email of such a conversation fills the task picker with it, "(from conversation)", with a tooltip saying where it came from. A task picked by hand, then one `#` task, win over it; emails of different tasks fill nothing and the picker names the tasks; taking the email off empties it. Drafts keep it.
- **An email captured before** (release 5.93's line) is matched on its Message-ID when both have one.
- **`email-ids [--dry-run]`** (KB decisions 27 and 34): reads each saved email's identifiers from its `.msg` and records them, and makes the rows of emails captured before release 5.74, so the conversations already captured teach their tasks. It never changes a `.msg`.
- **The troubleshooting report**: the selected email's identifiers by presence and layout, its key by its first 8 characters only; and **Also try a new email and a reply** (`--try-t-mail`), which makes a new email, a Reply all to the selected email and one to the newest saved `.msg`, reads their conversation indexes and closes them without saving, for checks C1, C3 and C4 before release B.
- **`t_mails`** is made, for releases B and C.

**Tests:** `tests/test_conversations.py` (new): the key and the index's shape, identifiers from a `.msg` and from transport headers, a save keeping them and the place, the latest filing winning, File to task, discards, a closed task, mixed tasks, a T-mail's filing, captured before by Message-ID, the backfill (dry run, `.msg` files unchanged, rows made for old captures, the command), schema 22 moving the places, and the report's lines and try-outs with a fake Outlook. `tests/test_conversation_fill.py` (new): the capture window's and the pinned window's picker. `tests/msg_builder.py` writes the identifiers.

**Built in the `k8-a` worktree** (2026-11-06), from `main` after 5.87 to 5.96, with 5.79 merged in when it reached `main` (its migration is 22, this release's 23). `tests/fixtures/roots/v22.zip` is the sample data root at version 22, made by 5.79's own code (`63b21e8`). Merged to `main` as `3882f3c` (tag `v5.101`) and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice); its corporate test is the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-101-test.md`.

## Decisions (releases 5.87 to 5.96)

The owner took every recommendation of this work in chat on 2026-11-06 ("I approve all recommendations"):

- **The Open allow-list** (5.87): `.doc`, `.xls`, `.xlsb` and `.ppt` stay openable, relying on Office's Protected View; the macro-enabled modern types are never opened.
- **A what-if's Delete stays Delete** (5.91): it holds no record of the owner's and has no Bring back, so Discard would promise what it can't do.
- **Save's Undo only for a capture that made nothing else** (5.91): a save that made a task, changed a field, put something on an agenda, made items, was split, or added someone to the people list has no Undo.
- **Outlook places in `app_state` for now** (5.93): they move into `capture_emails` with K8's release A, at the first schema change after 5.79's version 22 is on `main`.
- **`TaskEditDialog.__init__` left whole** (5.95): split when it's next touched, as its sections share some 20 locals.
- **The consolidation plan's decisions 386 to 397** as recommended (the design workspace's `docs/mods/consolidation-plan.md`), for releases 5.97 to 5.100.

## Release 5.95: the large UI classes, in smaller parts (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 12 (F11). No change in what DocRepo does, no schema change and no new package.

- **`BrowseWindow` in modules (`docrepo/ui/lists/`).** Its 112 list-view, page-command and notes methods moved, each with the comment lines above it and its text unchanged, into mixin classes it inherits: `base` (what every list shares: state marks, Find, the level buttons and folds, grouping, the command bar's buttons), `tasks`, `today`, `agendas`, `people_labels`, `documents` (with Search), `captures` (Unfiled and Discarded), `activity`, `planning` (the Timeline's, Capacity's and Priorities' hooks), `page_commands` and `notes_pages`. Its module-level helpers went to `lists/common.py`, its logger keeping its name, and `browse_window.py` imports them back and lists in `__all__` the names other modules and the tests read there. `browse_window.py` is 1,828 lines (from 3,745). A script did the move from the syntax tree, and ruff then took out each module's unused imports.
- **Mixins, not functions of (browse, tree, model), and not a view at a time.** The actions file said the builders "use only self.tree.add_row, set_columns and self.model()"; they also use the window's command bar, filters, selection and dialogs, so turning them into functions would change their code. The move keeps every method as it was, and `DocRepo/CLAUDE.md` now asks for a module per new list view or form, and welcomes a mixin's method turned into a function whenever it's touched. All the families moved at once, checked by the screenshots and one full run, rather than one release each.
- **`RowTree.building()`.** A context manager (and `is_building`) in place of the reaches into `_building` from outside the class; `add_tick` uses it too.
- **Named sections.** `SettingsView.__init__` (256 lines) calls `_build_data_root`, `_build_look`, `_build_hotkeys`, `_build_backups`, `_build_calendar`, `_build_parse`, `_build_files`, `_build_outlook` and `_build_troubleshooting`, and `CaptureWindow._build` (285 lines) calls `_build_type_row`, `_build_task_row` and `_build_editor`: each section of the builder moved as it was, with `lay` (and `open_import`, which one section uses) passed in, after a check that no section reads a local another sets. **Not done:** `TaskEditDialog.__init__` (338 lines): its sections share some 20 locals (the form's fields, its grid and rows), so splitting it means reshaping it, not moving it; it's left for when it's next touched.
- **A view's own state, kept without loading the model again (`AppContext.keep_state`).** The full run of 5.87 to 5.90 failed one test: since release 5.90 the browse window uses the shared model, which loads again on any write to docs.db, and a fold, a Timeline shift or a panel's place is a write to `app_state`, which no model reads. So a fold loaded every table again, and worked out a what-if's picture again. Those writes (the browse window's, the lists', the notes page's folds and the Today side panel's) now go through `keep_state`, which keeps the shared model as it is, as `rolled_back` does for a what-if's picture.
- **Checked by screenshots.** The design workspace's `review_shots.py` drew every window off screen, before the move and after it, from copies of one sample root: 83 shots, every one the same, pixel for pixel, except a 13-pixel spot where the top search box's clear button shows or doesn't, which differs between two runs of the same code too, and Settings, which shows the data root's path.

**Tests:** no new tests: the move changes nothing they check. The browse window's, capture window's, Settings', Timeline's and Today panel's test files pass. One narrowed run (`-k` over five files) failed `test_a_n_and_u_open_the_capture_window_on_the_selected_task` here and on `main` alike: an ordering quirk of focus under the offscreen platform, not this release's.

## Release 5.94: where typed text lands (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 11 (F13). The owner agreed to leave the placement rules alone for a month of daily use before the notes workspace's shared sections (decision 341) are built, and to make one rule simpler. No schema change and no new package.

- **The rule (`notes_page.place_note_where`).** New text goes at the end of the page. A note's heading goes under the page's heading with the same words (case and spacing aside) only when that heading is the page's last heading; then its lines go at the page's end, under it. A match higher up no longer pulls the text up into the page: the note goes at the end under its own heading, as typed. Lines under none of the note's headings go under the day's heading, as before, which is the page's end unless a later day follows. From 5.69 to 5.93 a heading went under the last match anywhere on the page, which could put new text in the middle of a long page without the owner seeing where.
- **The footer says which, before Save (`CaptureWindow._lands_where`).** "Save adds this at the end of Installation's notes (a Seals heading is higher up)." when a match is higher up; "Save adds this to the notes of Installation, under Crane, the last heading there." when it goes under the last heading; and, for text under no heading, as before ("… under Fri 6 Nov 2026"). Save's message names the headings it went under, as before.
- **Recorded** in `SPEC.md` 6.11 and the design workspace's notes workspace plan (its section 3 and decision 345), with the date the rule was taken.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_landing.py` (new): a heading matching one mid-page goes at the end, one matching the last goes under it, a new heading at the end, loose lines under the day, and the footer's three sentences. `test_notes_page.py`'s test of decision 345 now holds the new rule, and a second save under the last heading.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 to 5.93, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.93: the email path (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 10 (F14). No schema change and no new package. Outlook isn't on this computer: everything here is tested with fakes, and needs the corporate computer's hand test (the handoff should cover a reply chain, filing an unfiled email, and a second drag of one email).

- **A safe match to Outlook's selection (`outlook.match_places`).** A dragged email takes the EntryID and StoreID its own .msg carries (`PR_ENTRYID`, `PR_STORE_ENTRYID`, read by `msg.read`), when it has them; Outlook's drag-out copy rarely does, so it's checked first and the rest follow. Else the selected email with the same subject and the same sent minute (`selected_places` now reads `SentOn`, which the security guard doesn't cover). Else the one selected email with that subject. Never by subject alone when two selected share it, as a reply chain does: the email is left unplaced, with why (`EmailItem.place_note`), and the Saved notice says "couldn't tell which it was, and didn't guess". The old fallback by position stays only for one email dragged with one selected.
- **Marked on filing.** Each saved capture keeps where its emails are in Outlook (`captures.keep_email_places`), and **File to task** on an unfiled capture then marks them with the task's name, as a save does, under the same setting (`BrowseWindow.mark_emails`), saying how many in the status line. The places are kept in `app_state` (`outlook_places:<capture>`), not in `capture_emails`: a new column needs a schema change, and the `tagged-labels` branch's release 5.79 has taken schema version 22, so a migration here would clash with it. They move into `capture_emails` at the first schema change once 5.79 is on `main`. Captures saved before this release kept no places, so filing one of those marks nothing and says so.
- **An email captured before.** A dropped or added email that a live capture already holds, the same sender, subject and sent minute (`captures.captured_before`), is said in the Items panel: "“Crane booking” is already captured on Rigging plan, Thu 8 Oct. Open C000025." The link opens that capture's page in the browse tool (`AppContext.open_capture_page`). Save keeps it again all the same, as the owner may want it twice. Removing the email, or a new capture, takes the line away.
- **A 200 MB cap (`drop_target.MAX_DROP`).** A file or email a drag hands over above 200 MB is refused with one sentence, "Turnaround video.mp4 is 201 MB, over DocRepo's 200 MB for a dropped file, so it wasn't taken", and isn't read at all when the drag's own list of files gives its size; one with no size on the list is let go once its length is known. A `.msg` dragged from File Explorer above it isn't read either. A file from File Explorer is a path, copied later through SafeFS, and has no cap. `tree_files.MAX_FILE` stays where it is, the pattern this one follows.

**Tests:** `tests/test_email_path.py` (new): a reply chain matched by sent time and never guessed, a subject alone when only one has it, the place a .msg carries, the unplaced email said when marking, a save keeping its emails' places, filing an unfiled email capture marking it with its task (and not touching Outlook with the setting off), a second drop of one .msg saying where it is, a 201 MB drop refused unread, and a big .msg file refused unread. `tests/msg_builder.py` can give a .msg an EntryID.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 to 5.92, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.92: the `**` syntax, found and loud when it fails (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 9 (F6). No schema change and no new package.

- **The `**` list.** Typing `**` at a line's start, or after a space, opens the list `@` and `#` use (`items.typing` knows `**` as a third sigil; `items.star_options`): the kinds first (task, note, update, decision, event, closed, in progress, parked, not started), then every field, then the reference fields named in Settings, each with its example as the row's second line ("Field: **due, 5 Nov"). Typing narrows it by any of a name's names ("done" finds `**closed,`); the comma ends it. Choosing one puts in `**name, `. A name that's gone (`**date`) isn't offered.
- **The examples (`items.STAR_EXAMPLES`)** are one per name, used by the list, the Syntax tab and README. A test reads each on a task with nothing filled (title and description under a new task's first line, since a task always has a title) and finds no problem in any.
- **The Unread line card.** A line with a `**` that starts no operator (`**Tom to book the crane`, "Then **check the slings") gets a card in the Items panel saying "This ** line wasn't read; check its name and comma", with the line; a click goes to it. Save keeps the line as it is. `**bold**` isn't one, and a name with its comma that isn't a field (`**colour, red`) has its own card, "No field called colour", as before. `Reading.unread` holds them; the panel shows itself for them too.
- **A new person, visible.** A name after `@` that isn't on the people list gets a ticked "Add Zed to people" card. Unticked, it's kept in the cards' changes (`items.PEOPLE_KEY`), the item stays and is for no one yet, the new task's own line drops the name too, Save adds no one, and the tick holds for each part of a split. Ticked, as it starts, Save adds them as before. `Reading.new_people` holds them.
- **The keys' Syntax tab.** The Keys dialog (F1) is two tabs, Keys and Syntax. Syntax has six worked lines (a task for a person with a due date, a decision, a closed update, a `#` note, a split, `**due,`), every `**` name with its other names, what it makes or fills and its example, made from `items.OPERATORS` at run time with the capture window's reference fields, and what Parse and populate reads with no `**`: dates in words, names, hours, the state words (from the operators) and the event words. The other keys list Q, the Timeline's Quick edit.
- **README.** The Items bullet teaches the comma form (`**closed, …`, `**decision, …`, `**event, …`, `**due, 5 Nov`), which the period form it taught had stopped matching, and a new section, "Every `**` name", has the table `items.readme_table` makes. A test fails when README's table isn't that, or the period form comes back. The content text's placeholders say to type `**` for the list.

**Tests:** `tests/test_star_syntax.py` (new): typing `**`, the list's order, narrowing and the reference field, every example read with no problem, unread lines and what isn't one, a name that isn't a field, a new person's tick and unticked saving no one, README's table. `tests/test_star_syntax_ui.py` (new): the list in the capture window and a pick putting in `**due, `, the Unread line card, the new person's tick, and the Syntax tab naming every operator.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 to 5.91, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.91: errors you can see, and Undo where it was missing (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 8 (F12). No schema change and no new package.

- **The Problems line (`docrepo/ui/problems.py`, new).** A save whose files didn't all go is still saved, and its problems were only in a six-second tray balloon. Now the session keeps them (`AppContext.problem_log`), and the capture window's footer and the browse window's status line each show "2 problems with the last save", as a link, until it's clicked. The click lists them, with Show in File Explorer for the capture's folder. The balloon and the log stay as they were; nothing new is written.
- **Undo on Today and the agendas.** Done and Remove on Today, and Discussed and Remove on an agenda, end with Undo in the status line for ten seconds (`say_undo`, release 5.76's), which opens the item again as it was (`agenda.reopen_item`, `edits.reopen_agenda_item`); its task's status log reads the agenda's own rows, so the closing line goes with it. Done makes no task update itself (its "Update the task too?" opens a capture the owner saves or not), so Undo puts it back on Today and takes the question away. Discussed's Undo takes away the Update capture it opened as well, when nothing was typed or added in it; otherwise that capture stays open, and the status line says so.
- **Save's Undo.** For ten seconds after a save, Undo in the browse window's status line, and in the capture window's footer when it's opened again, discards the capture with the reason "undone" and opens its form again: the window keeps the form as a draft just before the save, the save moves that draft to Done as ever, and Undo brings it back and opens it. Nothing is deleted: the capture is in the Discarded view. Only a save that made a capture and nothing else has it (`save.can_undo`): no task made or changed, nothing put on an agenda, no items, no split, no one added to the people list, no capture filed. Taking those back as well is a product choice the owner hasn't made, so those saves have no Undo; a note on a task with no file is no capture (release 5.69), so it has none either.
- **One set of verbs.** Discard for a record (a task, a capture, a draft), Remove for a link and Clear for a field or the content text, in `GLOSSARY.md`. "Take out from under *parent*" is Remove from under *parent*; a template row's Take out… is Remove…; a tree file's preview says Remove; a what-if's Take off and Back on Today are Remove and Add back; a table's Delete row and Delete column on a notes page are Remove row and Remove column. Bring back stays as Discard's opposite. **Kept, and for the owner:** a what-if's Delete stays Delete. A what-if holds no record of the owner's (nothing changes until it's applied) and has no Bring back, so Discard would promise what it can't do; the owner can say otherwise. The `tagged-labels` branch's Back on Today (a removed tagged label, release 5.79) needs the same change when it's merged: Add back, as in a what-if.
- **New task under this one,** for New subtask, on the task page, its More menu, the Task view's More and palette, the Activity view's chip and a task's story. The browse tool mockup's own words changed with it (`activity.js`, `model.js`, `taskpage.js`, `taskview.js`), and `tests/fixtures/stories.json` by the same swap, which is what its export gives now; the published mockup isn't rebuilt.
- **The tooltip pass.** The task page's Note said "Add a note to this task's raw text log"; since 5.69 it goes on the notes page, and the tooltip says so. The capture window's sentence for a note with files on a task says it "keeps this as a capture on Installation, with its block on the notes page". The three windows hold none of the glossary's retired words (checked).
- **One double-click rule.** A double-click on a Timeline bar or name opens Edit task, as everywhere else; Q opens Quick edit for the row selected, and the card's Quick edit button has Q in its tooltip. Neither is offered in a what-if, since both change the real task.

**Tests:** `tests/test_problems_undo.py` (new): a save with a problem keeps the line showing in both windows until it's opened, with its folder; none without; Undo after Done, after Discussed with the untouched Update gone, and with typed words kept; Save's Undo discarding the capture and opening it again, then saving; no Undo for a save that made a task; the footer's Undo. `tests/test_glossary_words.py` (new): no New subtask, Take off, Take out or Back on Today in `docrepo/ui/`, `activity.py`, `stories.py` or `detail.py`. `test_timeline.py` and `test_timeline_whatif.py`: the double-click and Q. Tests that named the old words updated.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 to 5.90, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.90: quicker saves and edits (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 7 (F5). No schema change and no new package.

- **Only the pages a change reaches.** `pages.pages_touched` names the tasks whose Task.md can show something of the touched ones: themselves, the tasks tied to them along the ties' chain (a move follows its ties), each one's ancestors (a parent's Subtasks table) and descendants (inherited labels, Parents), the tasks it's tied to, its agenda items' targets and subjects, open or closed, and the task whose notes page holds its task line. It's asked of the model from before the change as well as after, since a move, an untie or a closed item takes a link away. `save_capture` (its picked task, the task it made, its agenda targets, its and its items' tasks, and a filed capture's old tasks) and the everyday edits (`edit_task`, `change_task`, `set_states`, `clear_reminder`, `close_agenda_item`, `add_to_today`, `close_today_item`, `edit_today_note`, `file_capture`, `edit_capture`, through `edits.write_touched_pages`) pass theirs. The rest keep the full rebuild: the daily work, `rebuild`, the Excel import, Settings, templates, tree files and the rarer edits.
- **Contents.md** is made with every write and written only when its text changed, as every page is. It lists every task, so deciding whether a change reaches it would be the same work as making it.
- **The model's indexes.** `notes_of`, `copies_of` (new), `versions_of` and `status_log_of` read indexes built once per `Model` on first use (captures by task, saved copies by task, versions by task, the status log by task and the agenda by subject), in place of a scan of every capture, copy or status line per task; `files_of`, `raw_log_of` and `summaries_of` use them. A Model is a snapshot nothing changes after it's built (checked: no code assigns into its lists), so they can't go stale. Each returns a list of its own.
- **One model in the browse tool.** `BrowseWindow.model()` is the context's shared model, loaded again only once docs.db has changed (release 5.52's rule), so its own copy and the five places that cleared it by hand are gone.
- **Measured** on the large sample (`load-sample --large`: 34 tasks, 475 captures, 508 documents), best of three:

| | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| One save (a note on the task with the most captures) | 164 ms | 97 ms |
| One Edit task (its current status) | 177 ms | 57 ms |
| The daily work's model and every page | 120 ms | 63 ms |

  The large sample has few tasks; the saving grows with tasks times captures, since the full rebuild asked every capture once per task.

**Tests:** `tests/test_touched_pages.py` (new). The oracle: after each of 15 kinds of change (a note, a new subtask, an update closing a task, an item made for another task, a quick task on Today, an agenda note, a parent renamed, a task moved, a label on a parent, states from a row, an agenda item closed, a task put on Today, a reminder cleared, an unfiled capture filed, a capture's title fixed) and a move along a chain of ties, a full rebuild finds no page left to write. A note on a 200-task project rewrites only its task's Task.md. A page is the same built alone or with all. The indexed helpers give what the old scans gave, with those scans kept in the test as the oracle for one release.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 to 5.89, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.89: data integrity (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, item 6 (F3). No schema change and no new package.

- **`check` (`docrepo/check.py`).** The `check` command, and **Check the data root** in Settings' Troubleshooting card, list as Markdown: captures whose `capture.md` is missing or can't be read, each naming the finished draft in `System\Drafts\Done` last changed up to 30 minutes before the capture's save, which may still hold its words; documents whose text was read and whose `extracted.txt` isn't there; saved copies not in their task's `Files` folder; captures and documents missing from the search index, or whose entry isn't what it would be now (the index keeps no time, so "older than its capture" is "not what it would hold now"); and SQLite's `quick_check`. Each line says what the owner can do, and nothing is repaired. The list is kept as `System\check-<date>.md` and shown in the troubleshooting report's window, which now takes its title and opening line. The command exits 2 when something needs looking at. On the sample it finds the three saved copies `load-sample` doesn't make, which is right.
- **capture.md before the commit.** `save_capture` writes the capture's `capture.md`, and each item's, at the end of its transaction. A write that fails raises `SaveError` ("capture.md couldn't be written (PermissionError), so nothing was saved. Your capture is still here: try Save again in a moment."), the transaction rolls back (no capture, task, note on the page or ID used), and the capture window keeps the capture as it does any `SaveError`, so its draft isn't moved to Done. Emails, attachments, extracted text and saved copies are still written after the commit, each failure a problem as before: none of them is the only copy of anything. If a `capture.md` is written and the commit then fails, the next save takes the same ID and folder, and writes it again.
- **Checked backups.** Each new copy of docs.db is checked with `PRAGMA quick_check` before it replaces its slot, and the log line says "checked". One that fails isn't kept: the slot keeps the last good copy, the failed copy stays as its `.tmp` (the next backup writes over it), the files are mirrored all the same, the log line says "database copy not kept", the last backup's time isn't moved (so the next start tries again), and Settings' backup card says what happened in red until a backup makes a good copy. A backup that can't be made at all (no drive) shows there too. `copy_before_upgrade` (5.88) is checked the same way.
- **Drafts in the backup.** `System\Drafts`, with each draft's `draft.json`, `.msg` files and pictures, is mirrored to `Files\System\Drafts`; the rest of `System` (docs.db, the lock, the logs) still isn't. The rules file says so.
- **A restore, tested.** A sample data root is backed up, its mirror copied into a new folder, the backup's database copy put in as `System\docs.db`, and `rebuild` run, as `backup-log.md` says; the tasks, captures and Installation's notes page are as they were, and `check` finds no capture missing.

**Tests:** `tests/test_integrity.py` (new): a clean sample, a missing capture.md named with its draft, a missing extracted text and search entry, the command and its kept list, a save whose capture.md can't be written saving nothing and then saving, a checked backup, a copy that fails its check, the drafts in the backup, and the restore. `test_browse_window.py`: the backup card's problem line and Check the data root; `test_backup.py`'s message says "checked".

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87 and 5.88, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.88: safer upgrades, and the test configuration (2026-11-06)

From the review's agreed actions, items 4 (F8) and 5 (F10). No schema change and no new package.

- **Migrations split as SQLite reads them (F8).** `db.statements` splits a script where `sqlite3.complete_statement` says a statement ends, so a semicolon in a string, a comment or a trigger's body no longer cuts one in two (migration 13's comment had to avoid one). `executescript` isn't used: it commits the open transaction first, and every migration runs in one transaction with the others. Every migration so far splits into the statements it had before.
- **A copy before an upgrade (F8).** When docs.db's version is below the current one and a backup folder is set, `connect` copies it first to `Database\docs-before-v<old>.db` in the backup folder, with SQLite's backup API from a read-only connection, through a SafeFS that allows only that folder (`backup.copy_before_upgrade`, sharing `run_backup`'s copy). With no backup folder, or one that can't be used, the log says no copy was taken and the upgrade goes on, since it's one transaction. The rules file lists the copy.
- **Upgrades tested from real old databases (F8).** `tests/fixtures/roots` has a zipped data root (docs.db and settings.json, about 60 KB each) at schema versions 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 17, 19, 20 and 21, each made by that version's own `load-sample` at the commit named in `roots.json`, with two made-up key dates from version 12. Each is upgraded in the tests, and its tasks, captures, notes pages and key dates are counted before and after, with SQLite's integrity and foreign key checks.
- **A damaged docs.db (F8).** A file SQLite can't read as a database raises DocRepo's `DatabaseError`, "docs.db is damaged; restore it from the backup folder. Its backup-log.md says how." At start-up it's shown in a window, as is a newer database's message, since `pythonw` has no console to print it to. A locked database stays SQLite's own error.
- **The test configuration (F10).** `conftest.py` marks every test in a module that imports `docrepo.ui` or PySide6 (read from its syntax tree) or sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORM` as `gui`, registered in `pytest.ini`. With `-m "not gui"` those modules aren't collected at all, so Qt is never imported. The cache is on again, so `--lf` works. Each run's temporary folder is `.pytest_tmp\run-<process id>`, and runs' folders older than a day are cleared, so two runs at once don't clear each other's. The temporary folder stays in the repo, as it has been since `pyproject.toml` went, in case the corporate computer's own temporary folder is the reason.

**Tests:** `tests/test_migrations.py` (new): each fixture upgraded whole, a quoted semicolon and a trigger, every migration split as before, a failed migration leaving docs.db as it was, the copy before an upgrade, no backup folder, an unusable one, and a damaged file. `tests/test_test_config.py` (new): the `gui` mark, the run's own temporary folder, and no Qt in the fast run.

**Measured:** the fast run, `pytest -m "not gui"`: 1026 passed in 129 s with the 5.79 suite running beside it.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), with 5.87, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.87: a release identity and guard rails (2026-11-06)

From the design workspace's whole-app review of 2026-11-06 (`docs/docrepo-review-2026-11-06.md`) and its agreed actions, items 2 (F9) and 3 (F7). No schema change and no new package.

- **A release identity (F9).** `__version__` in `docrepo/__init__.py` is the release, "5.87", and moves with each one; `--version` and the troubleshooting report already read it, and Settings' Look card has a Version row, "DocRepo 5.87". `CHANGELOG.md` is new, from this file's table: a line per release, newest first, with its date and one sentence; each release adds its line there as well as its row here. The README's status line says which release it is. Each release is tagged when it's committed (`v5.87`); tags reach GitHub with `git push --tags`.
- **The write and launch rules by test (F7).** `tests/test_hard_rules.py` reads each module's syntax tree, as `test_no_network.py` does, and fails on `shutil` or `subprocess` used at all, `os`'s writing, deleting and launching functions (named or called, so `(starter or os.startfile)` counts), `open` with a write mode, and `Path`'s writing methods (`write_text` and `write_bytes` with their one argument, `touch`, `mkdir`, `unlink`, `rename`, `replace`), outside four modules: `safe_fs.py`, `log_setup.py` (its handler's path comes from `checked_path`), `backup.py` (SQLite's backup API into the backup folder) and `opener.py`. SafeFS's own `write_text(path, text)` and `replace(src, dst)` take two arguments, `str.replace` two or three, and `date.replace(day=1)` none, so they pass. Its own tests show what it catches and what it lets through, and that a `shutil.copy` added to `captures.py` fails it.
- **File Explorer from `opener.py` (F7).** `opener.show_in_explorer` and `opener.open_folder` start Explorer with a list of arguments, never one string: `["explorer", "/select,", path]`, which Python quotes as Windows expects (`explorer /select, "C:\Docs\Lift plan.pdf"`). `ui/explorer.py` only passes them on, for the windows that import it. Both take a stand-in launcher for tests.
- **Ruff (F7).** `ruff.toml` selects pycodestyle, pyflakes, bugbear, blind excepts and bandit's checks, so the code's `noqa` markers name rules that are checked. Ruff isn't in `requirements.txt`; `CLAUDE.md` says how to run it from a venv of its own. What it found, and what was done:
  - **Real slips, fixed:** `templates.tree_words`'s `date` annotation named a type that wasn't imported (harmless only because of postponed annotations); `tokens.LIGHT` and `DARK` each had `pri_high` twice, the earlier value (the Timeline's) silently replaced by the later (release 5.81's priority edge), so the earlier one goes; loop variables named `items`, `tip` and `date` hid the module or function of that name for the rest of the method; four closures read loop variables late (each called in its own turn, so no wrong value was seen, but each now binds what it reads); 28 unused imports.
  - **Kept, with the reason in the marker:** 21 SQL statements that build a column or table name of DocRepo's own (values stay parameters; the review's smaller items add checks on those names), the silent `except` blocks around COM and the drop target, the sample's seeded random numbers, and `ElementTree` on a document's own XML, which already refuses a DTD (`defusedxml` isn't an approved package).
  - **Not checked:** line length (the code's lines run to 200 characters), `assert` (they narrow types and state invariants, and DocRepo never runs with `-O`) and `zip(strict=)`.
- **The Open allow-list (F7).** `.doc`, `.xls`, `.xlsb` and `.ppt` stay openable: the rules file now says they're opened relying on Office's own Protected View, and that the macro-enabled modern types (`.docm`, `.xlsm`, `.pptm`) are never opened. The owner can take the four out of `OPENABLE` instead, and the rule goes back to what it was.

**Tests:** `tests/test_hard_rules.py` and `tests/test_version.py` (new); `test_pins_opener.py` checks Explorer's arguments; `test_browse_window.py` the Version row.

**Not as the actions file said:** the version goes in the troubleshooting report, which is `docrepo/diagnose.py`, and it was there already; `docrepo/report.py` is the task model's report, compared line by line with the mockup's test vectors, so it's left as it is.

**Built in the `review-fixes` worktree** (2026-11-06), from `main` at `e3bc57d`, committed as `1ba1690`, merged to `main` and on GitHub the same day, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

## Release 5.86: the task page's clean-up, stage T4 (2026-11-06)

The last stage of the task pane review (`docs/ui-design/task-pane-review.md` in the design workspace): R9, ways down a long page, with decision P7 (Jump to, the sticky title and the landmarks yes; the auto-fold as an option off by default, since nothing in the field folds by age and a folded day is hidden content). Built at the owner's word, without a mock. No schema change and no new package; nothing in docs.db's pages, `Task.md` or search changes.

- **Jump to** (`TaskPane.show_jump_menu`, `jump_to`, `latest`; `NotesEdit.outline`, `go_to_end`): a `QMenu` from the Notes header's new button (`jump`, an arrow down to a line, `docrepo/ui/icons/jump.svg`), the sticky line's Jump to, or Ctrl+G (a `QShortcut` on the pane, so it works from the notes and the facts alike, at the caret when the notes have it). It lists the page's headings and day dividers in order, each indented an em per level under its day, the owner's headings in Semibold and the days in the regular weight, with Top of the page first (not in the Task view's tab, which has no facts) and Latest last. A choice unfolds what hides the heading (`go_to_block`), puts the caret at its end and scrolls the heading to just under the sticky line, once at once and once more after the page has laid out what unfolded. Latest puts the caret at the end of the notes and brings it into view.
- **The sticky title line** (`TaskPane.sticky`, `set_title`, `_place_sticky`, `_sticky_check`; `Pages.task_parts` adds `title`): a child of the pane over its viewport, not of the scrolled page, shown while the scroll value is past the facts row's bottom and hidden otherwise, on every scroll and on the page's own resizes; the title in Body Semibold, cut with "…" to the room left by the two buttons and whole in its tooltip; `QWidget#stickyTitle` opaque on the content colour with a stroke under it; Jump to and Latest as subtle buttons in the accent. `notes_only` (the Task view) hides the facts, so the line never shows there.
- **Landmarks** (`LandmarkBar`, the pane's vertical scroll bar since this release; `NotesEdit.landmarks`): a 2 px line across the bar for each heading that shows, at its share of the page's height (`bar_y`), the owner's headings in the accent and the days in `scroll_hover`, drawn over the handle; a tooltip names the headings within 4 px (`marks_near`, up to six). The marks are read again a moment after the notes' layout changes, a fold changes, or the page's height changes as a group or the history opens (an event filter on the page for its resizes and layout requests), and on the pane's resize.
- **Fold days older than two weeks** (`NotesEdit.fold_old_days`, `OLD_DAYS = 14`; the Notes menu's checkable `old_days_action`; `BrowseWindow._notes_menu("old_days")` and `load_notes`): per task, kept as `old_days` in `page_folds:<task>` beside the groups' folds, off until the owner ticks it. Ticking it folds every day heading before today less 14 days at once and says how many ("Days older than two weeks fold when this page opens: 44 days folded now."), and `load_notes` folds them again whenever the page opens, after the owner's own folds are put back; unticking unfolds them. The fold arrow, Jump to or the caret opens one, and that fold is kept as any other. `_save_page_fold` takes the pane, so a Task view's page keeps its own task's choice.
- `SPEC.md` 8.4, `GLOSSARY.md` (Landmark, Jump to) and `docrepo/ui/icons/README.txt` follow.

**Measured** (`after-t4/` in the review's assessment folder, from the same roots; `task_pane_measure.py` gained `--old-days`, which folds the old days before measuring without writing to the root, and records the landmarks' count): nothing moves on a page that fits a screen (the sample's project is still 854 px, 1.1 screens, with 4 landmarks). On the large sample's 96-capture tie-in page (54 days, 7,272 px, 9.3 screens, 54 landmarks), the option folds 44 days and the page opens at 2,850 px, 3.6 screens, with the last fortnight's ten days open; the 15-capture site survey, whose every day is older than two weeks on the sample's date, opens at one screen of folded days. The review's last target ("the tie-in page's open part: the last fortnight") is met when the option is on, which is the owner's per task.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_edit.py` (the editor: the outline lists every heading and the landmarks only those that show, in order; folding the old days before a cutoff counts what changed, leaves the owner's own fold alone and unfolds on request; Latest lands at the end. The pane: Jump to's entries in order with Top and Latest, a jump scrolls the heading to the top with the caret on it, the sticky line shows past the facts and goes at the top with the title in its tooltip, Latest, the landmarks' names and order and the tooltip's nearness, the old-days choice folding on, remembered per task, not on another task, opened by Jump to, and unfolding off; the Notes menu's four entries), `tests/test_task_view_notes.py` (the menu's entries). The full run: 2659 passed, 1 skipped.

**Built 2026-11-06 in the `task-pane-t1` worktree, merged to `main` (fast-forward) and on GitHub** the same day, at the owner's word, without a hand test. **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-86-test.md`.

## Release 5.85: the task page's clean-up, stage T3 (2026-11-05)

The third stage of the task pane review (`docs/ui-design/task-pane-review.md` in the design workspace): R3 (provenance in the margin, not in the text; decision P2) and R5 (day headings as quiet dividers; decision P3). Built without the mock and without R14's Qt WebEngine spike, at the owner's word to go straight on: the page's own editor turned out to do what R3 asked without a new engine or a new page format. No schema change and no new package, and **nothing in docs.db, `Task.md` or search changes**: the caption is still a line of the page; only the editor's drawing of it does.

- **Captions tuck behind a mark** (`NotesEdit.tucked`, `mark_of`, `mark_at`, `mark_name`, `_paint_mark`, `MARKS`, `MARK`): a caption with a line under it is hidden by `refold` (as a folded section's lines are), and the page paints its mark, an icon for its kind (a note, an email, a file, an update, a new task, a decision, meeting notes), in the gutter beside the block's first line, in the scroll colour and the secondary colour on hover; the caption's words are the mark's tooltip; a click on it opens the capture (`open_ref`). A caption with nothing under it stays a line, so it has somewhere to be, and tucks when a line is typed under it. The review had planned a new block attribute for this (`R3`, size L); hiding the caption line and drawing from it costs nothing in the data and keeps the Captures tab, Discard, Add it again, merge and `Task.md` exactly as they were.
- **Around a tucked caption:** Backspace at the start of its block's first line takes the caption off, with the same message as before (the text is untied; Ctrl+Z puts it back); the Captures tab's On the notes page (`go_to`) lands the caret at the start of that line, without selecting it; a block's tucked caption moves with it, and past a captioned block as one unit (`move_lines`, `_unit_start`); the arrow keys pass over it as over a folded section (`_skip_hidden`); the folded pill still counts it as a capture.
- **Day headings as dividers** (`_block_format` and `_char_format` take ``day``; `style_block` and `_put` read it with `notes_page.day_of`): a heading that reads as a date is Caption Strong in the secondary colour, with 12 px above it rather than 16 and 2 below rather than 4; the owner's headings keep their sizes. A heading edited into or out of a date changes with it. Folding, Alt+Shift+D and the folds kept per task are as before.
- `SPEC.md` 8.4 and `GLOSSARY.md` (Caption) follow. Group by day as a per-task option (the review's R5) isn't built: with the dividers quiet, the owner can say whether it's wanted.

**Measured** (`after-t3/` in the review's assessment folder): on the sample's project the notes page's own height goes from 461 px to 290 (the captions' 64 px and half the day headings' 104 gone), the whole page from 1,025 px (5.84) to 854, 1.1 screens, against 1,761 before the review; the large sample's 96-capture page from 9,237 px to 7,272, 9.3 screens, against 20,017 (25.6) before; a leaf's whole page fits one screen. On the page the only dates printed are the day dividers; the captions' dates and times are in the marks' tooltips. The notes' first words: 528 px on the sample's Installation, 357 on a leaf.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_edit.py` (a caption with a line under it is hidden and its mark found by the block and under the pointer, named by its kind, a click opens the capture, the jump lands on the line, the day heading is Caption size and the owner's isn't, Backspace at the line's start takes the caption off and Ctrl+Z puts it back, the page round-trips unchanged; the Captures-jump and moving-lines tests brought up to the tucked caption, a block now moving with it), `tests/test_task_view_notes.py` (Enter on a note lands at the line under its mark). The full run: 2655 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed on assertions that put the caret on the caption (`test_browse_window.py`'s Go to it on the page, and `test_state_menu.py`, which read the old top view); both brought up to date and pass, with the changed files run again: 65 passed. 2657 pass in all, 1 skipped.

## Release 5.84: the task page's clean-up, stage T2 (2026-11-05)

The second stage of the task pane review (`docs/ui-design/task-pane-review.md` in the design workspace): R1 (the notes as the body, the facts a header, the history behind one click), R2 (the header as a strip of summary lines that open, built as widgets, R14's first half), R6 (one History, folded), the owner's stage two (the status log behind the status; Files on the pane, decision P10). No schema change and no new package.

- **The page in parts** (`Pages.task_parts`): the head (the path, title, chips, the new **fact line** and the description), the status card's pieces (`status_parts`), the status log (this task's story), each group's summary and body (`split_section` takes a section's heading apart from its body, so the group's button carries the title and its summary keeps the count, the bar and the link), the Files group (`files_body` with `FILES_SHOWN`, then "All n in the Task view"), the history (`HISTORY_TABS`: Captures and Summaries) and, from 760 px, the rail. `task_page` still draws the page as one, for reading and the tests. The fact line (`fact_line`): Due (or Closed, Next due, Next meeting, Deadline, No due date) with its days, then up to three people; `when_who_summary` names the other dates and the hours.
- **The header** (`docrepo/ui/task_header.py`): `TaskHeader` holds the head (a `FitPageView`, now in `page_view.py` with `set_margins`, so each part keeps the page's left edge without a document margin's height above and below it), the status card (`QFrame#statusCard`, its chevron opening the status log, newest first), a `FlowLayout` strip of the closed groups' heads, and one `Expander` per group (a `QToolButton#groupHead` with its chevron and title, a Caption label with the summary's words while closed, the summary in full as HTML while open, and the body). Toggling a group moves its head between the strip and its own row (`_place`). Key dates, Agenda and Subtasks start open (`OPEN_BY_DEFAULT`); which groups are open, and the status log, are kept per task (`app_state`, `page_folds:<task>`, `BrowseWindow._page_folds`, `_save_page_fold`).
- **The pane** (`TaskPane`): the facts row (the header, with `top` as the rail beside it when wide), the Notes frame, then the **History** expander with `bottom` as its body, closed until opened and remembered. `views()` lists every HTML part for the browse window to wire; `layout_changed` fires from the pane itself when its width crosses 540 or 760 px (the hidden rail can't); `text()` reads the header, the rail, the notes and the history. `notes_only` (the Task view) hides the facts and the history. The history reads newest first (`PageState(order="new")` in the browse window).
- **Old links keep their meaning:** `fold:details` opens the Details group, `tab:log` the status log, `tab:files` the Files group.
- `SPEC.md` 8.4 follows.

**Measured** (`after-t2/` in the review's assessment folder): on the sample's project the notes' first words move from 603 px (5.83) to 563, the page from 1,685 px to 1,025 (2.2 screens to 1.3), the facts above the notes from 475 px to 435, of which the open Subtasks group is 166; a leaf with nothing open starts its notes at 397 px; the large sample's 96-capture page goes from 22 screens to 12 with the history folded; the facts' type treatments are 5 or 6. The first-screen target (320 px) holds only with Subtasks folded, which the owner can do per task.

**Tests:** `tests/test_detail.py` (the page in parts: the fact line, the status pieces, the groups' summaries and bodies, the history's tabs, the Files group, the rail), `tests/test_browse_window.py` (closed groups in the shared row, opening and folding, the status log, the history, the folds remembered per task and in docs.db, the old links), and the earlier tests brought up to date (`test_notes_edit.py` reads the header; `fold:details` and `tab:log` open their groups). The full run: 2655 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (`test_state_menu.py` read the state link from the old top view; it reads the header's head part now and passes). 2656 pass in all, 1 skipped.

**Built 2026-11-05 in the `task-pane-t1` worktree, merged to `main` (fast-forward) and on GitHub** the same day, at the owner's word, without a hand test. **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-84-test.md`.

## Release 5.83: the task page's clean-up, stage T1 (2026-11-05)

The first stage of the task pane review (`docs/ui-design/task-pane-review.md` in the design workspace: the owner's reevaluation of the task preview pane and the notes corpus, from a brainstorm memo of 2026-11-05, decisions P1 to P10 taken as recommended the same day). T1 holds the recommendations that change no page format and need no decision: R7 (one rule for time), R8 (chrome on demand), R4 and R4b (one line for a wordless capture, no repeated description, a task made from the notes as one line), R10 (two weights) and R11 (the notes' line length). 5.82 is the no-Due plan's, in its own worktree. No schema change and no new package.

- **One rule for time (R7).** The status block's corner shows the date the text last changed; how long ago is its tooltip (`Pages.status_block`). A subtask's row shows its due date alone, red once passed, with "Due Wed 14 Oct, in 6 days" in the tooltip (`subtask_section`, from `due_of` rather than the lists' `due_cell`). Due, Deadline, Starts, Reminder and key dates keep their day counts in one form (`days_words`). The page's time formats go from ten to eight; the two relative forms left ("in 22 days", "3 days ago" in tooltips) are the decision fields' and the tooltips'.
- **Two weights (R10).** `section()` wraps its count and link in `.secx` (regular weight), so "3 of 4 open" and "New subtask" are no longer bold by inheritance from the heading; `.sk` (the status block's heading) and `.over` (an overdue date) lose their bold; `.rel` and `.sub` say their weight; the history's entry and chapter titles use `.ttl` (600) rather than `<b>` (700). In `tokens.CSS` and `detail.CSS` alike. Measured on the sample's project with the review's `task_pane_measure.py`: the facts' distinct (size, weight, colour) treatments go from ten to the playbook's six (title, Body, Body Strong, Caption, secondary Body and the accent), the chips' tints apart.
- **Chrome on demand (R8).** The Checklist summary is drawn only when the page has a checklist line (`ChecklistSummary.changed`, `TaskPane.summary_box`): Ctrl+1 or the Checklist button makes the first and the summary appears with it. The format bar and the Table button show while the caret is in the notes (`TaskPane._card_focus`), and stay `BAR_LINGER_MS` after it leaves so a click on the Highlight or Heading menu keeps them (the bar's buttons take no focus; the menus do); the ⋯ menu is always there. The frame's fixed chrome before the first block goes from 142 px to 66.
- **The notes' line length (R11).** `NOTES_LINE` = 90 characters: the editor's maximum width is 90 average characters of the Body font plus the document margins (552 px for Segoe UI Variable at 10.5 pt), left in the row with a stretch after it; the frame's header and summary keep the frame's width. The review asked for 75, which is 462 px at this font, narrower than the usual pane's 628 px of text; 90 is Butterick's top and still half the wide page's 1,000.
- **One line for a wordless capture (R4).** `notes_page.block` writes no caption for a capture with no words of its own beyond its files, whose caption would say only "File", "Note" or "Update" (`captionless`); a caption that names an email, a change, meeting notes or where an item came from stays. Typed text that only repeats a file's description (the capture window's description became both) isn't written. Such a capture is on the page while any of its file lines is (`on_page`, given the stories to read its text; without them, Task.md's reading, when every file line is there), `find_block` and `remove_block` take its documents (`refs`), `edited_blocks`, `remove_capture` and Add it again pass them, `merge` brings a captionless block in by its new document lines under the day it sits under, `NotesEdit.go_to` lands on the first file line, and the browse window and the Task view pass the capture's documents (`_refs_of`).
- **A task made from the notes is one task line (R4b).** A new line kind, `[Task: Rigging plan · Mike Ortiz · due Wed 14 Oct](task:T000031)` (`task_line`, `task_line_text`, read as `Line("task")`). `block` finds the items made from a capture (`from_notes`, `item == "task"`), turns each item's own page lines (`note_markup` of its capture.md) into one task line in their place (`task_lines`), so the rule holds when a page is rebuilt or a block added again; a note that's no capture (5.69) gets its task lines from `edits.add_note`'s `made`, which `save` fills from the items it made. The item's text stays in its capture and on the task's own page. In the editor a task line is drawn and handled as a file line (the accent, a hand cursor, a click opens the task, typing goes to the line below, Backspace removes it, no heading on it) and written back as itself; `Task.md` prints it as a link to the task's Task.md. Stage 26's task lines (a tick box that closes the task) build on it.
- **Pages built before 5.83 keep their lines**: the rules apply to blocks added from now on, and the first-page build (`build`) follows them for a data root that hasn't built pages yet.
- `SPEC.md` 6.11 and 8.4 and `GLOSSARY.md` (Caption, Task line) follow.

**Measured** (the review's scripts, over fresh sample roots built with this release, in `docs/ui-design/assessment/task-pane-2026-11/after-t1/`): on the sample's project the notes' first words move from 679 px to 603 of a 783 px screen; the large sample's pages go from 1,787 lines to 1,493, captions from 471 to 280, blocks with no words of their own from 165 to 77 (the 77 left are emails and updates, whose captions say something), repeated descriptions from 103 to none, dates printed from 809 to 618, and the 96-capture tie-in page from 25.6 screens to 22.1. The sample's own pages don't change: every capture in it has words.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_page.py` (five more: a repeated description isn't written; a captionless block is on the page, comes off with its capture and comes back; a note with words keeps its caption; a task from meeting notes is one line on the meeting's page, its words on its own page, printed as a link in Task.md; a task from a note that's no capture), `tests/test_detail.py` (the status date and tooltip, the subtask date and tooltip, no "<b>"), `tests/test_checklists.py` (the summary only with a checklist, the bar hidden, the notes' width), `tests/test_task_view_notes.py` (the bar on focus). The full run: 2651 passed, 1 skipped, 3 failed, each on the old wording or the always-shown bar (`test_checklists.py`'s width bound, written for the real font and not the offscreen platform's wider one; `test_open_links.py`'s Links heading; `test_visual_stages.py`'s narrow-pane bar, now fitted with the caret in the notes); those were brought up to date and pass, and the changed files run again: 139 passed. 2654 pass in all, 1 skipped.

**Built 2026-11-05 in the `task-pane-t1` worktree, merged to `main` (fast-forward, 2654 passed, 1 skipped) and on GitHub** the same day, without the owner's hand test (their choice: on to the next stage). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-83-test.md`.

## Release 5.81: a task's priority as its row's left edge (2026-11-05)

Asked for in chat: tasks with a priority should show it in every task view. The owner chose, from a trial of three ways and their own idea, to colour the strip at the row's left edge by priority (the list rows review's decision L9, `docs/ui-design/list-rows.md` in the design workspace). It changes decision 77 (rows showed only Critical). 5.79 and 5.80 are the tagged labels plan's.

- **The edge** (`RowTree.drawRow`, `row_priority`): an open task's bucket, in its colour, at any depth: Critical `pri_critical` (red), High `pri_high` (amber), Medium `pri_medium` (blue), Parking lot `pri_parking` (grey), new tokens each 3:1 or more on white and on the hover tint, with dark values. Not ranked and closed tasks have none. A row is a task's when its key is (`task`, ID), so Today's items, reminders, key dates, the lists, the Task tree, Agendas, Activity's task rows, Search and Documents' tasks all have it. The browse window works out every open, ranked task's bucket as it fills a list (`browse_window.priorities_of`, into `RowTree.priorities`), so drawing a row never reads the database: the first full run found a window left from an earlier test, painted after its database had closed, failing when an earlier version loaded the model to draw the edge. A row kept for context has it faded. The selected row keeps its accent bar: the trial's inset pill would have lain over a top-level row's chevron, and the page beside it names the priority.
- **The top-level task's colour leaves the lists' rows** (`EDGE_ROLE` and `tree_view.edge` are gone): the edge means one thing. Projects keep their colours on the Timeline, the Priorities board's cards and the capture window's item cards (decision 9).
- **Named on hover** (`HtmlDelegate.helpEvent`): over the edge, "Priority: High"; elsewhere on the row, its tooltip with that line added, as colour alone carries it on the row.
- **Critical keeps its chip** beside Urgent: the rarest, most important bucket keeps words too.
- **The Today side panel's rows** have the same edge (`today_panel.edge_of`, `_EdgeFrame`): Today's items, and the reminders and Due in the next 7 days rows.
- **`SPEC.md`** now says what 5.78 changed in the lists (List rows, A row's actions, the state as an icon, People's aliases) and this release's edge; 5.78 had left it out.

**Tests:** `tests/test_list_rows.py`, 9 more: a ranked subtask's row has each bucket's colour at its edge, read from the drawn pixels; an unranked top-level row has no edge and no project colour; the selected row keeps the accent bar; a closed or unranked task gives no priority; Today's rows and the side panel's have it; Critical keeps its chip; drawing a row reads nothing from the database. The full run: 2649 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

**Hand-tested by the owner, merged to `main` and on GitHub** (2026-11-05). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-81-test.md`.

## Release 5.79: tagged labels (2026-11-05)

From the design workspace's tagged labels plan (`docs/mods/tagged-labels-plan.md`, items 102 to 105, stage 30), asked for in chat on 2026-11-05: "I want to start labelling equipment tags and compiling those as a list of 'Taggedlabels' in a metadata field called 'Tagged labels'", found in everything captured, with a glossary imported from Excel or CSV. The owner took decisions 365 to 381 as recommended and approved the mock (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/tagged.js`). Schema version 22. Tag patterns, Suggested and Never a tag (items 106 and 107) are release 5.80.

- **The glossary (`docrepo/tagged.py`, schema version 22).** `tagged_labels` holds each tagged label, its **key** (its letters and digits in capitals, so V-110 and V110 are one label), description, how it came (an import, a pattern in 5.80, or by hand), where from, and whether it's in the glossary or suggested (5.80). `tagged_aliases` holds its aliases, in order. No IDs: a label is known by its key.
- **Matching with no setup (decisions 368 to 370, `Finder`).** A label, or a tag-shaped alias (a digit and no space), is found however its parts are joined: a hyphen, an en dash, an underscore, nothing, or a space between capitals. So V-110 is found as V110, v-110 and V 110, but "page 101" isn't P-101. A label with fewer than four letters and digits (E-12) must be in capitals with no space, so "e12" and "v1" aren't tags. Any other alias ("feed drum", "CT fan 1") is found as whole words, in any case. P-204A is its own label, and "P-204A/B" mentions P-204A and P-204B. A tag inside a longer one (AV-110, V-1100) isn't found.
- **What's read (decision 366).** Each capture's and document's text in the search index, without its ID line, and without a capture's title when its content has it. Each notes page's own text, without its captures' blocks (from a caption to the next caption or heading), which are read as their captures, so a capture's words count once. Each task's own fields (title, description, current status, deadline description, key dates' descriptions), read from the model as they are.
- **Kept current (decision 377).** `search._put` and `search.index_pages` index each item here too (`tagged.index`): its tag-like words as written go into `tagged_words` (runs of letters and digits joined by one mark each, with a letter and a digit), and what they mention into `tagged_mentions`, with how often and as written. A change to the glossary matches every item's kept words again (`rematch`) without reading any text, apart from word aliases.
