Both tables are caches: `search.rebuild` rebuilds them (`tagged.rebuild`), and a data root from before 5.79 builds them on the first import. The glossary is read once per version, by a token in `app_state` that every change makes new, so a save that builds the model several times reads 20,000 labels once.
- **The owner's own changes (decision 376).** On a capture's or document's page, × removes a tagged label from that item, with Undo in the status line, and it stays removed when the text is read again. **Add** puts one on by hand, from the glossary, or a new one added to it first. Both are kept in `tagged_choices`, apart from what's found, by key; a removed one is listed under the chips with **Add back**.
- **Where it shows (decision 367).**
  - **A capture's and a document's page:** a Tagged labels row, once there's a glossary, of plates in the mono font (`.tg`), so a tagged label doesn't read as a task's label, each with its count, ×, and the description as its tooltip.
  - **A task's page:** a Tagged labels row in Details, from its own fields, notes page, captures and their documents, and the documents it holds, each item once, the most mentioned first.
  - **Task.md:** a Tagged labels row after Labels, "R-101 (2), V-110 (1)", once there's a glossary, so the AI layer can read them. capture.md doesn't change.
  - **The Task view:** `tag:V-110` (with the space) is a typed filter, shown as a chip, keeping the files, emails, notes and subtasks that mention it; a notes page section by its own words.
- **The Tagged labels view (decision 379),** under Manage, with the `tagged` icon (an equipment tag's plate): each label with its description quiet after it (the list rows' recipe), its aliases and how many items mention it. **Import…**, **Export…** and **Add…**. A label's page: its description and aliases, how it's found (its own spellings), how it's written and how often, when and where it came from, and every item that mentions it, newest first, under each top-level task, with the words around the mention. **Edit…** changes its description and aliases, which are checked: an alias that's only digits, or that's another label's, is refused, saying why.
- **Import (decision 371, `docrepo/tagged_import.py`, `ui/tagged_import_dialog.py`).** An `.xlsx` (read-only, through `excel_import.read_sheet`, which takes a row limit now) or a `.csv` (UTF-8, else Windows' own encoding; a comma, semicolon or tab), up to 100,000 rows. Each column fills the tagged label, its description or its aliases, or is left out, guessed from its header ("Tag No.", "Service", "Other names") and changed in the form. The preview says what's added, which descriptions differ, the aliases added, what's merged and moved, and what's refused; nothing is written until Import. Importing again merges: new labels are added, aliases added, and a description that differs replaces DocRepo's unless **Keep DocRepo's descriptions** is ticked. Nothing is removed. Two rows that are one tag written two ways become one label, the other spelling its alias. A row that's another label's alias becomes a label of its own, and that label loses the alias: the list wins. An alias that's only digits, or that two labels share, is refused, once.
- **Export (decision 371).** The glossary as a CSV in the import's three columns (Tagged label, Tagged label description, Tagged label aliases, separated by "; "), written to `Exports` in the data root under its date and name, as tree files' Export does, since DocRepo writes nowhere else, and copied. With a BOM, so Excel reads its accents. The rules file says so.
- **Search (decision 378).** A tagged label typed however it's written ("r101", "v 110", a word alias whole) is the first group, **Tagged label**; "P-204" lists P-204A and P-204B. When it names one label, what mentions it is found too, in its own group, even where it's written another way.

**Found in the trial** (`dev_data_tagged`, offscreen, with Windows' fonts), and changed:
- **A capture's words were counted twice:** once on the capture, and again in its block on its task's notes page. A page is now read without its captures' blocks.
- **The view drew 20,000 rows in 8.2 s.** It draws at most 2,000 (`ROWS` in `docrepo/ui/lists/tagged.py`), with "And n more. Search finds any of them" under them, and a glossary bigger than that starts with **Mentioned only** ticked: 0.28 s.
- **A save with 20,000 labels took 0.9 s, not 0.1 s:** the model, built several times in a save, read the whole glossary each time. Now it's read once per version: 0.11 to 0.14 s.
- **The list showed only the label and its count** in a narrow list, as the list rows hide columns after the title; the description goes on the row, quiet, as People's aliases do.
- **Measured with 20,000 made-up labels:** reading the file 0.02 s, the preview 0.10 s, Import with matching everything 0.37 s, a change to the glossary 0.01 s, a label's page under 0.01 s.

**Not built yet:** tag patterns, the tester, Suggested and Never a tag (5.80); descriptions and fuzzy matching (stage 32, set aside, decision 380). A word typed into a capture's block on a notes page counts for the capture's own text, not the page's.

**Tests:** `tests/test_tagged.py` (new): the matching rules (joined however, a space between capitals, short tags, a tag inside a longer one, pairs and suffixes, word aliases), the kept words, the import's guesses, preview, merge, moves and refusals, an `.xlsx` and a semicolon CSV, what's indexed found when the glossary comes, a save matched at once and a new label found from the kept words, a notes page, a task's own fields, the owner's own changes after a rebuild, a discarded capture, checks on adding and editing, search, Task.md's row, the pages' chips and a label's page, Export, a 20,000-row import, a capture counted once though its block is on its page, and the spellings. `tests/test_tagged_ui.py` (new): the view empty, Import through the form, a label's page and Task.md, × with Undo and Add back, Add from the glossary or new, Edit and a chip's link, a refused alias, Export, search's first group, the Task view's `tag:` filter, and a big glossary's Mentioned only. `tests/test_db.py` and `tests/test_nav_pane.py` know version 22 and the new view.

**Built in the `tagged-labels` worktree** (2026-11-05), from `main` at `ad8e096`, before the owner's hand test. Committed to the `tagged-labels` branch on 2026-11-06, after a full run there (2677 passed, 1 skipped), and not merged to `main`. The full run: 2675 passed, 1 skipped, and 2 failed that expected the old layout (`test_email_description.py` schema version 21, `test_templates_ui.py` Templates third under Manage); both updated, and their files pass (41), so 2677 passed, 1 skipped.

**`main` merged into the branch** (2026-11-06, with 5.81 and 5.83 to 5.96). As release 5.95's rule asks, the browse window's tagged labels code moved into a module of its own, `docrepo/ui/lists/tagged.py`, as functions of the window (the view, a label's page commands, Import, Export, Edit, Add, and × with Add back and Add on an item), with one-line hooks in `browse_window.py`, `lists/page_commands.py` and `lists/documents.py`. 5.79's changelog line sits between 5.81's and 5.78's, as the changelog is in release order, so `__version__` stays 5.96, the highest. Ruff's one finding (a query joining only `?` marks) is marked. The version 21 root in `tests/fixtures/roots` covers the upgrade to 22. The full run after the merge: 2805 passed, 2 skipped, and 1 failed, as release 5.91 retired "Back on Today" in the windows: a removed tagged label comes back with **Add back**, Remove's opposite, and the glossary words test then passes. **Merged to `main` and on GitHub** (2026-11-06, the owner's word), tagged `v5.79`.

## Decisions (release 5.79)

- Decisions 365 to 381 of the tagged labels plan, as the owner took them (recommended) and approved in the mock.
- **Export goes to `Exports`,** not a folder the owner picks: DocRepo writes only in the data root. It's copied as well.
- **The view starts with Mentioned only ticked when the glossary has more than 2,000 labels,** and draws at most 2,000: the plan's list, made usable at the corporate list's size.
- **Capture window:** nothing new, as the plan asks for none.

## Release 5.82: no Due that wasn't given (2026-11-05)

Asked for in chat on 2026-11-05: "Items without due dates should default to no due date, and therefore shouldn't show up on the today list", and then "when I create a meeting as a container for a bunch of notes and tasks... The item containing that meeting shouldn't show up as a task on my today list. There might be several subtasks DEFINED by the meeting, and those should definitely show up". The design workspace's `docs/mods/no-due-plan.md` has items 109 and 110 and decisions 382 to 385, from the owner's words. No schema change and no new package. 5.79 and 5.80 are the tagged labels plan's, and 5.81 is priority on rows (the list rows review's decision L9).

- **What it was.** A task made in the capture window, Today's box or from an item got no Due unless one was typed or read. Two things gave one anyway. Meeting notes made each new meeting, and this time's meeting of a regular meeting, with today as its Due, In progress until closed by hand (release 5.8), so every meeting sat in Due in the next 7 days and then in Overdue. And Parse and populate read "today" anywhere in the text as a date, so "Spoke with Tom today about the seals" or an item "@Tom check the crane anchors today" was due today; an Update did the same to its task.
- **A meeting's day is its Start (decision 383).** `save_capture` makes the meeting with today as its Start and no Due. `schedule.held_on` is a one-off meeting's day: its Start, with no Due, Deadline or recurrence. `schedule.windows` makes such a meeting that one day, so it stays on the timeline and off the "no dates" list. `Model.meeting_when` gives that day, so Add to and the agenda picker don't say "no date set". The task page's When says "Meeting" and the day, with no countdown and no "No due date".
- **Meetings off the due lists (decision 384).** `lists._due_soon` leaves out a task with Meeting ticked, whatever its dates, as it leaves out routine tasks. That's the Overdue and next 7 days list, its count, Today's Due in the next 7 days and the Today side panel's, so meetings made before 5.82, with today as their Due, leave them too, with nothing changed in docs.db. The tasks under a meeting count by their own dates.
- **"Today" alone is no date (decision 385).** `parse.py` (from the mockup's `parse.js`, changed the same way) drops a bare "today" or "tonight" before Due and Deadline are chosen, unless something makes it a date to meet: "by", "before", "until" or "till" just before it, "due" before it in its clause, "deadline", "no later than" or "at the latest" around it, or "must", "ahead of" or "prior to" before it. "EOD today", "a week from today", "starting today" (a Start) and "Remind Tyler today…" (a Reminder, and the Due when there's no other) read as before. The test vectors' Parse and populate section, about 140 captures and 100 date phrases, comes out byte for byte the same.

**Tests:** `tests/test_no_due.py` (21): a new meeting's Start and no Due, with its item's "by Friday" kept and "today" not; the meeting off both lists and its dated item on them; its day in `held_on`, `meeting_when`, `windows`, the timeline and the task page's When; this time's meeting of a regular meeting; a meeting with a Due, as made before, off the lists while its dated subtask stays and its undated one doesn't come on; a task saved with no Due has none; fifteen sentences with "today", for New task and Update; "starting today" and a reminder. `tests/test_meeting.py`'s regular meeting now has a Start and no Due, and `tests/test_typing_lag.py`'s card test gives its line a Due with "by today". Full run: 2659 passed, 1 skipped, and the one failure, that card test's bare "today", passes with "by today" (its file and the related ones, 572, rerun).

**Committed to the `no-due` branch** (2026-11-05), before the owner's hand test, not merged. It was built as 5.81 while priority on rows was built as 5.81 in the `row-priority` worktree, which was committed first, so it was renumbered 5.82 before this commit. Merged to `main` as `37bb3d4` and on GitHub 2026-11-07, without a hand test (the owner's choice).

**Not checked yet:** the owner's hand test, and the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-82-test.md`.

## Release 5.78: list rows (2026-11-05)

From the design workspace's list rows review (`docs/ui-design/list-rows.md`), asked for in chat: Today and Unfiled looked cluttered, capture IDs weren't needed, a folded Task tree was all bold, and rows came in different heights. The owner took its decisions L1 to L8 as recommended. No schema change and no new package: the rows are painted with Qt's own delegate, as the Task view's (release 5.34) already were. 5.69 to 5.77 are the notes workspace's and round 7's.

- **One line, one height per view (L1).** `RowTree.set_density` sets each view's row height from `ROW_DENSITY` in `browse_window.py`: 28 px where rows nest (the Task tree, the lists, Labels, Templates, Documents' tasks), 32 px elsewhere, with uniform row heights. Word wrap is off. Agendas, Documents, Activity and Search give a row with a second line 48 px (`TWO_LINE_ROW`), its headings keeping the view's height; an agenda item with no note keeps 48 px, its title in the middle. Before, the sample's rows came in seven heights, from 28 to 78 px.
- **A painted first column.** A row's first cell can be a `Line` (`tree_view.py`): an icon, a lead, the title, chips, quiet words and a context, plus a second line. `HtmlDelegate._paint_line` paints it on one line, and `_parts` cuts it to fit in turn: the context first, then the quiet words, the lead, the chips and the count, and the title last, with "…", never dropped. Every other cell is still HTML, now on one line in the middle of the row and cut with "…" when it doesn't fit (`_paint_html`). `Line.html` keeps the same words in `HTML_ROLE`, for Find, the tests and the Classic look. After a list is filled, each column after the title widens to its widest cell, up to 220 px (`_size_columns`), so a date isn't cut.
- **No bold task rows (L2).** Top-level tasks are Regular in the new look; headings that aren't tasks keep Body Strong. The colour edge stays.
- **The state as an icon (L7).** The state's icon (`state-ns`, `state-ip` in the accent, `state-pk` in parked's colour, `state-cl`) goes before the title, with its word as the icon's tooltip (`HtmlDelegate.helpEvent`). The new look has no State column (`_cols` and `_shape`); a key date, a reminder, a note and Done today have icons of their own. Round 7's state menu (item 101, 5.76) should open from this icon.
- **Today's context (L3).** After the title, in quiet words: its project (`views.project_text`, the top-level task) and "since Tue" for a carried item. No "Top-level task", "A note" or boxed chip; the whole path is in the tooltip. The Today side panel's second line does the same.
- **No IDs in rows (L4).** `unfiled_rows` drops the capture's ID from its sub-line, `discarded_rows` drops "Task T…" and "Capture C…", and Search's rows lose their leading IDs. The IDs stay in tooltips and on the page. Find in this view adds a row's key to what it searches, so an ID still finds its row. Unfiled's source is an icon rather than a column.
- **Marks on the line.** Urgent and Critical stay chips (painted with 4 px corners); a repeat, "2 parents" and labels are quiet words joined with " · ". A deadline is a red ◆ before the date, not a "deadline" line under it.
- **Actions (L5).** On hover only, 28 px icon buttons (`ACTION_ICONS`: Edit, Note, Done, Remove, Clear, Discussed, Add to Today, Make it a task) at the row's right end, over its date, each with its words as its accessible name and at the start of its tooltip. The row's words stop short of them (`strip_left`). The current row keeps its date in view; its actions are on its right-click menu and their keys, as before. This fixes release 5.59's strip, which lay over the title.
- **Headings (L6).** No column headings on Today, Unfiled, the lists, the Task tree and Search (`NO_HEADINGS`). Documents, People, Agendas, Activity and Discarded keep theirs. Documents' task rows no longer put a due date under Status.
- **A folded parent's count (L8).** A parent's open subtasks, as a small neutral badge after its title, only while it's folded.
- **The Classic look** keeps its wrapping rows, bold top-level tasks, State column and links, with the new words (no IDs, no "Top-level task").

**Tests:** `tests/test_list_rows.py` (21): every row in seven one-line views is that view's height, headings too, with the whole tree open; a long title is cut with "…"; two-line views' items are 48 px, an agenda item with no note too; no task row bold, folded or open, and headings still bold; Today's context is the project, with no filler words, and the path in the tooltip; Unfiled and Discarded show no IDs, and Find still finds a capture by its ID; the state's icon and only Title and Due columns; a parent's count, shown only while folded; the deadline's ◆, a repeat as quiet words and a date column wide enough; the context going before the title is cut; the Classic look's words. Tests that read the old cells (`test_browse_window.py`, `test_key_dates_ui.py`, `test_visual_stages.py`, `test_views.py`) now read the `Line`, the date's new column and the buttons' accessible names.

**Hand-tested by the owner and committed to the `list-rows` branch** (2026-11-05). **Merged to `main`** the same day, the owner's choice of order ("merge 5.78 first"), after `main` (5.69) was merged into the branch: the full run 2583 passed, 1 skipped, none failed. **On GitHub** (2026-11-05). Round 7's 5.76 (a task's state from its row, built in the `round7` worktree at the same time) makes the State column's cell a button and takes `UserRole + 5` for it (`STATE_ROLE`), which is `LINE_ROLE` here: so `round7` moves the state menu onto the row's state icon (decision L7) and renumbers its role when it merges `main`. **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-78-test.md`.

## Release 5.77: every capture window fresh, and Drafts (2026-11-05)

Round 7's item 94 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-7-plan.md`), asked for in chat on 2026-11-05 ("I want every capture window opened to be a new, fresh capture window. If I close a window that has content that hasn't been saved, I want it to be available to edit as a draft"), with decisions 333 to 338, all as recommended, built from the mock the owner approved the same day (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/round7.js` there). It changes the rules file. No schema change and no new package.

- **What it was.** There was one capture window, and X, Esc and Alt+F4 only hid it, with everything still in it, so every way of opening it showed the same capture again. Its words went to `System\capture-draft.json` every 30 seconds and came back after a restart. The Capture wheel's blank capture (release 5.41, decision 215) was the one way to start fresh: it set the open capture aside, one only, in `System\capture-draft-aside.json`, and its files were kept only while DocRepo ran.
- **Every open fresh (decision 333).** Closing the window files what it holds in Drafts, without asking: X, Esc, Alt+F4, the main hotkey on the window in front, and the pinned window's Esc and ✕. The window then empties, so the next open starts empty. A notification says "Kept in Drafts". Holding nothing, or only a picked task, it just closes. `hideEvent` does it, except when the window is pinned (pinning hides it), saving or minimized (a spontaneous hide).
- **While it's open (decision 334, the mock's rule).** `show_capture` takes `keep`:
  - **Kept as they are:** the main hotkey, the tray's New capture and a type's hotkey bring the window forward or change its type. So do the pinned window's open-in-full and F12, which are the same capture.
  - **Afresh:** a command for a task, a meeting, some text or Make it a task (the browse tool's N, T and U, the wheel's Meeting) puts what's there in Drafts first, and the footer says "Your other capture is in Drafts."
  - **The wheel's blank capture** is an ordinary open (decision 337). A capture with something in it goes to Drafts, and a picked task alone is cleared. `"blank": false` keeps the open capture, as the type hotkeys do.
- **The list (decision 335).** A **Drafts** button in the footer, with how many there are (not counting the one open), shows only when there are some, in the row with More and Save at their height. It opens a list above it (`draft_ui.DraftsList`): each draft's first words (else its title, its first file's name, or Meeting notes), then its task, its files and when it last changed, the newest first, as 48 px two-line rows. A click opens one, putting what the window held in Drafts first ("The capture you had is in Drafts."). × discards it. The tray menu has **Drafts…**, and the palette has Drafts.
- **The folders (decision 336, `docrepo/drafts.py`).** Each draft is a folder in `System\Drafts` in the data root, named by when it was first kept ("2026-11-05 104233"). It holds `draft.json`, with the whole form and a row for each file, and beside it:
  - a dragged email's `.msg` under `emails`, written as handed over (`SafeFS.write_copy`), with its EntryID and StoreID in `draft.json` for Outlook's category;
  - a file a program handed over under `files`, written the same way;
  - a pasted picture as its PNG (`SafeFS.write_picture`, through the picture's own save).

  Paths and links stay as text. A restart keeps a draft whole. The email is read again from its `.msg` (`msg.read`), and its parts get their descriptions, choices and Keep back, by name, else by place. An email whose `.msg` DocRepo can't read keeps its bytes and comes back from its fields. A file the folder already holds isn't written again, and an unchanged capture isn't written at all. A row's file name can't leave its draft's folder (`drafts.file_path`).
- **Done.** A draft saved as a capture, or discarded, moves to `System\Drafts\Done` (`SafeFS.move`), as DocRepo deletes nothing; the owner can empty that folder by hand. Drafts never expire (decision 338). Discarding has Undo in the footer for ten seconds, which moves the folder back. Clear still empties the window without making a draft, and its Undo now brings the files back too.
- **What it replaces (decision 337).** The 30-second keeping goes on, into the open capture's own folder, so after a crash or a restart it's in Drafts and the window opens empty. The app's Quit keeps it too. On the first start, `capture-draft.json` and `capture-draft-aside.json`, when they hold something, move into Drafts as drafts of their own (`drafts.move_old_drafts`), each named by when it was saved. A draft set aside before kept no files, so opening it says which to add again. `docrepo/ui/draft_aside.py` is gone; its form code is in `docrepo/ui/drafts.py`.
- **The words:** the footer's hint, the key list, the help text, the pinned window's ✕ and the palette say Esc closes the window and anything unsaved goes to Drafts. The default wheels config's comment on `capture.new` says what it does now.
- **The rules file** says where drafts' files are written, and that a finished draft moves to Done, never deleted.

**Tests:** `tests/test_drafts.py` (new) covers:
- closing to Drafts and the next open empty, Esc, and a picked task alone just closing;
- the main and type hotkeys keeping the capture, a command for a task starting afresh, one with only a task in the window moving nothing, and the wheel's blank capture;
- the list's rows, opening a draft while the window holds another, and a saved draft moving to Done;
- discarding with Undo, and Clear with Undo bringing files back;
- a restart keeping a draft whole: an email's `.msg` with an attachment's description and a part unticked, its EntryID, a file a program handed over, a pasted picture, a path and a link, saved after the restart as before;
- an unchanged capture not written again, the old draft files moving in (and an empty one staying), and a stored name that would leave its folder;
- the pinned window's Esc and open-in-full, the tray's Drafts…, and an empty list.

**Changed for 5.77:**
- `test_meeting_window.py`: the draft kept in its folder, and found in Drafts after a restart.
- `test_pin_window.py`: the pinned window's Esc makes a draft, which is opened and saved from it.
- `test_wheel_window.py`: the set-aside tests replaced by two for the blank capture as an ordinary open.
- `test_hours_a_week.py`: the form's h a week through `drafts.form_of`.

The touched modules' tests and the capture window's other tests, in the worktree beside 5.74 to 5.76 (not the full run): 999 passed, 1 skipped. Checked by eye on Windows' real platform, with the app's look: the footer's Drafts button, and the list with two drafts, one from today and one from the day before.

The full run, with 5.74 to 5.76 in the same worktree: 2612 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

**Round 7 merged to `main`** (5.74 to 5.77, 2026-11-05), the owner's word, before the owner's hand test, with `main`'s 5.69 and 5.78 merged into `round7` first (2639 passed, 1 skipped). **On GitHub** (2026-11-05). **Not checked yet:** the owner's hand test, and the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-74-test.md` to `release-5-77-test.md`, in that order.

## Release 5.76: a task's state from its row (2026-11-05)

Round 7's item 101 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-7-plan.md`), asked for in chat on 2026-11-05 ("the ability to click on a task's state ... and have a dropdown open so that I can impromptu change the task state"), built from the mock the owner approved the same day (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/round7.js` there), with decisions 360 to 364, all as recommended, and the mock's two open points settled as mocked. No schema change and no new package.

- **The menu (decision 360).** `docrepo/ui/state_menu.py` builds it for every place below:
  - **The states:** Not started, In progress, Parked and Closed, each with its mark (`state-ns` and so on, in the task page's colours). The current state is ticked, and it's where the keyboard starts. Arrows, Enter and Esc work, as it's a `QMenu`.
  - **The tick:** Qt draws no tick beside an action that has an icon, so `StateMenu` paints one after the current state, in the accent (`icons/tick.svg`, new), where the mock has it.
  - **Closing a branch:** a task with open subtasks also gets "Closed, with its n open subtasks" (decision 364).
- **The State cell as a button.** In the browse tool's lists, a row's State cell is a button (`RowTree.mark_state`, `STATE_ROLE`):
  - On hover, a 4 px-corner backplate in the pressed colour sits round it, with the chevron after the words, and the pointer is a hand. Both stay while its menu is open.
  - A click opens the menu under the cell, and doesn't pick the row. A quick second click isn't a double-click on the row.
  - Its tooltip says "In progress. Click to change the state."

  `BrowseWindow._mark_states` marks, after each list is drawn, the cells that show their row's task's own state, on:
  - the task tree;
  - Today, with its due rows;
  - the agenda;
  - the sidebar's lists (Overdue and next 7 days, Waiting on others and the rest).

  A key date's, a reminder's or a context row's cell stays words.
- **Where else (decision 361):**
  - **Every task row's right-click menu** in those lists gets a **State** submenu after its other commands (`RowTree.row_menu`), which is also how the keyboard reaches it, with the menu key. Activity's task rows have it too.
  - **The Task view's task rows:** the state at the right of a Subtasks row is the button (`ResultDelegate.state_rect`, `ResultList.state_clicked`). A right-click on any row gives its commands, and a task row's State submenu (`ResultList.row_menu`).
  - **The Today side panel's rows:** the state is a small button with its chevron, shown when the panel is wide, in place of the state's words in the second line.
  - **The task page:** the state under the title is a link with its chevron always shown (`state:` links, `Pages.state_button`), as a page has no hover. A discarded task's is only its chip.
  - **The Priorities board's cards and the timeline's card:** a right-click gives Open task, Edit task… and the State submenu (`card_menu`). There's no button there, as settled with the mock. Not in a what-if, as Quick edit and Update aren't.
- **Saving (decision 362).** A pick saves at once through `edits.set_states`, as Edit task does:
  - a status log line as "Browse tool", which Activity lists with the other changes made outside a capture;
  - no capture;
  - one transaction, and the pages written once.

  Picking the state a task already has changes nothing.
- **Undo (decision 363).** The status line says "Closed: Rigging plan." with an **Undo** link after it, for ten seconds (`say_undo`). Undo sets each task back, logged too, and says "Set back. The status log keeps both changes." A list of open tasks drops a closed one at once, since the list is drawn again. The Task view's footer and the Today side panel say it there too, each with Undo (`state_menu.UndoButton`).
- **A whole branch (decision 364).** "Closed, with its n open subtasks" closes the task and every task below it at every level that isn't Closed or discarded (`open_below`), Parked ones too, as mocked. One Undo puts each back as it was. The line says "Closed: Installation and its 3 open subtasks."
- **Different from the mock:**
  - the task page's chevron is always shown, as rich text has no hover;
  - the Today side panel's state shows only while the panel is wide, as its second line does.

**Tests:** `tests/test_state_menu.py` (new) covers:
- the menu's states, tick, starting point and branch line, and none for a Closed task;
- `set_states` logged as "Browse tool" with no capture, its closed day, and Activity's line;
- a task row's State cell as a button, a click opening the menu without picking the row, and hover lighting it with a hand;
- Closed leaving the task tree and Undo bringing it back, with both status log lines, and the same state changing nothing;
- a branch closed and set back with one Undo;
- the State submenu on the task tree's and Today's rows, and key date rows left as words;
- the task page's `state:` link;
- the Priorities and timeline cards' menus;
- the Task view's task rows, with the footer's Undo and the right-click menu;
- the Today side panel's state button and its Undo line.

Checked by eye on Windows' real platform, with the app's look:
- the task tree with a state lit on hover;
- the menu, with its marks, tick and branch line;
- the task page's state;
- the status line's Undo;
- the Task view's Subtasks with a lit state and the footer's Undo;
- the Today side panel's row.

`tests/test_state_menu.py` with the tests of every view and module it touches (Activity, the browse window, the task page, edits, Priorities, the Task view, templates, the timeline, Today, the Today side panel, tree files and the visual stages): 563 passed. The full run comes with 5.75 and 5.77.

**After merging `main`** (5.69 and 5.78, 2026-11-05): 5.78 takes the State column out of the new look's lists and paints a task's state as an icon before its title, from the row's `Line` in the first column (the list rows review's decision L7, which names this menu). So the state menu opens from that icon: `RowTree.state_cell` tests the icon's 16 px square in the first column, and the rest of the row picks the row as before. `HtmlDelegate` draws the backplate under the icon (`icon_box`, `_plate`), and the icon's tooltip adds "Click to change the state.". `BrowseWindow._mark_states` marks the first column of each row whose icon is its task's own state, so a key date's, a reminder's or a note's icon isn't a button. `STATE_ROLE` is `UserRole + 6`, as 5.78's `LINE_ROLE` took + 5. The Classic look keeps its State column, whose word is the button, with its chevron, and the right-click menu's State submenu is as it was. `tests/test_state_menu.py` changed with it: the icon is the button and a click on the title picks the row, a key date due tomorrow has no button (the test found none before, so it checked nothing), and the Classic look's cell. The full run, with 5.69 and 5.78 merged in: 2639 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

## Release 5.75: the `#` list's marks (2026-11-05)

Round 7's item 92 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-7-plan.md`), asked for in chat on 2026-11-05 ("some form of clearer visual distinction assigned to parent level tasks in the dropdown list and to tasks that have subtasks", following the UI playbook), with decisions 327 to 330, all as recommended. Built from the mock the owner approved the same day (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/round7.js` there). No schema change and no new package.

- **The playbook's rules it follows:**
  - hierarchy from weight and colour before size;
  - the top-level task's colour as a dot on its own row, never tinted across its children;
  - leading marks on every row or none, lined up;
  - a chevron that only goes in, with a backplate under the pointer;
  - the task tree's 16 px indent.
- **A task's mark** (`_RefDelegate.parts`, `RefPopup.mark`). Every `#` row starts with an 8 px slot:
  - **A top-level task (decision 327)** has a filled dot in its colour, the task tree's and the timeline's (`task_colour`), and its name in Semibold. The grey "Top-level task" goes: it's in the row's tooltip.
  - **Every other task (decision 328)** has a 7 px ring in its top-level task's colour, and its name in Regular. Inside a level, a ring takes the colour of the first task gone into (`RefPopup._root`). Q4 overtime budget, under Installation and under Q4 cost reduction, is blue under Reactor impeller upgrade, as in the mock.
  - The capture window gives the list each task's mark (`CaptureWindow._task_mark`, `RefPopup.task_info`). The `@` list has none.
- **Open subtasks (decision 329).** A task with open subtasks ends with how many, in grey Caption, then the chevron in a 20 px box (`RefPopup.kids`, which replaces the yes-or-no `opens` cache). Under the pointer, the chevron and the back chevron get a 4 px-corner backplate in the hover colour and the pointer becomes a hand (`RefPopup.hot`, `chevron_at`, `mouseMoveEvent`, `leaveEvent`). A row's words for a screen reader and its tooltip say "3 open subtasks".
- **A level (decision 330).** The task heading it is a header row:
  - its own fill (the base layer's colour) and a hairline under it;
  - the back chevron, its mark and its name in Semibold;
  - "3 open subtasks" at its right end, in place of its path.

  Its subtasks are indented 16 px. Rows are 28 px tall, the tree's height (they were 24 px or the font's height and 8).

**Tests:** `tests/test_ref_marks.py` (new):
- a top-level task's dot, Semibold name and count, with "Top-level task" in its tooltip;
- a ring in the top-level task's colour, with the path;
- a level's header and indented rows;
- the two-parent task's colour inside a level;
- no mark on people;
- the backplate's chevron under the pointer, into and back, and gone on leaving.

`tests/test_ref_list.py` (release 5.65's) passes unchanged. Checked by eye on Windows' real platform, with the app's look: the top level, a level two deep with a hovered chevron, and a search.

## Release 5.74: the lists search past a space, and an email's description (2026-11-05)

Round 7's items 91 and 93 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-7-plan.md`), asked for in chat on 2026-11-05, with decisions 324 to 326, 331 and 332, all as recommended, built without a mock. 5.69 to 5.73 are reserved for the notes workspace.

- **The search stopped at a space (item 91).** The owner's "it will do a live search until I enter a space". `items.typing` read the word being typed with a pattern that needed a letter after each space, so `#Pump ` closed the list. The next letter opened it again, but `items.options` then matched only names starting with everything typed: `#seal pump` never found "Replace pump seals". After five words or 30 characters, the list closed for good. Now:
  - **Past a space (decision 324).** The list stays open after a space, over up to 8 words and 60 characters (`items.TYPING_LIMIT`). `# ` with nothing before the space stays a heading. The `@` list works the same way.
  - **How words match (decision 325).** Each word typed must start a different word of the name, in any order (`items._each_its_own`): "rigging plan r" doesn't match "Rigging plan", whose two words the first two took. A name's words are split at spaces and at anything that isn't a letter or a digit. A task's words may also come from the names on its path, with one word at least in its own name, so `#install pump` finds the Pump under Installation and not the one under Commissioning, and `#installation` doesn't list everything under it. A person's aliases count as their names.
  - **Ranking and closing (decision 326).** Whole names first, then names that start with what's typed (its space too), then names with every word, then those found through their path, each in its old order. A space after a whole name that no other name goes on from closes the list: `#Valve ` is done, while `#Pump ` stays open for "Pump station". The rule looks at every name, so `@Ana ` stays open for Ana Lopez, whose alias is Ana. A pick doesn't open the list again (`CaptureWindow._pick_end`, set before the pick goes in, as putting it in reads the list).
- **An email's description (item 93).** The owner's "captures of emails do not usually cause the description field to be activated for input". An email's own row had no description box, by design: an email is the capture itself. Only its parts had boxes, and the cursor went to the first kept part's. Most emails have no attachment, or only a signature picture that starts unticked, so the cursor went to the content text. Now:
  - **The box (decision 331).** Each email's row has a box, "What's this email about?", optional, as its subject already names it (`FileEntry.email_desc`, outside "Use the first file's description", which is for documents). After a drop, the Outlook button or a drag through DocRepo's own drop target, the cursor is in the first new email's box, attachments or not.
  - **The window comes forward.** A drop brings the window it landed on forward (`_come_forward`, the pinned window while pinned), since a drop on Windows leaves the keyboard in the program the drag came from. That's files, links, and emails through the drop target or Outlook. The Outlook email hotkey still leaves the keyboard in Outlook (release 5.33.1).
  - **Where it's kept (decision 332).** In `capture_emails`, schema version 21: one row per email, by its place among the capture's emails, with its `.msg` file (`msg_name`, or none when there was nothing to save), subject, sender, sent time and description. It's the table the knowledge base's K1 plans, started now; K1 adds Outlook's identifiers. The model has it as `Capture.emails` (`CaptureEmail`).
  - **Where it shows:**
    - the Task view's email row ("From Ann Example · Crane for the Tuesday lift"), found by its words;
    - the capture's page, a new Emails row with each subject, file and description;
    - capture.md's new `emails` list, only for captures with emails saved since;
    - search;
    - the notes page's caption ("Email from Ann Example: Crane booking – Crane for the Tuesday lift").

    Left empty, everything shows as before.
  - **Different from the plan:** emails captured before have no rows, not rows read from their `.msg` files at the upgrade. Docs.db's upgrades are SQL only, with no data root to read files from, and a backfilled row would show nothing new, as older emails have no description. K1's planned `email-ids` command covers them.

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_hash_search.py` (new):
  - what's typed past a space, and what ends it: a heading, two spaces, 9 words, 61 characters;
  - matching over a made-up tree: after a space, any order, each word its own, the path telling two Pumps apart, a path match needing a word of the task's own name;
  - the ranking, a finished name closing the list, 8 at most, and people with aliases;
  - in the capture window over the sample data: the list open past a space, any order and the path, a finished name and a sentence closing it, and a pick not opening it again until you type on.
- `tests/test_email_description.py` (new):
  - descriptions kept per email, an email with nothing to save having no file, and capture.md's `emails`;
  - the sample's older email captures with none, and schema version 21;
  - the Task view's row, search, the capture's page and the notes page's caption, with and without a description;
  - in the window: the box with the cursor for an email with no attachment, the first of two emails getting it, the description reaching the row, and a drop bringing the window forward while the Outlook button doesn't need to.
- **Changed for 5.74:** `test_layer0.py` (the cursor is in the email's own box now, not its first part's), `test_db.py` (version 21, and an empty `capture_emails` after upgrading), and `test_template_timing.py` (the schema at 20 or later).

Checked by eye on Windows' real platform, with the app's look: the capture window with an email's row, its subject and its box, and `#install rig` listing Rigging plan. The full run: 2579 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

## Release 5.69: notes land on the page (2026-11-05)

The first of the notes workspace's five releases, from the design workspace's `docs/mods/notes-workspace-plan.md` (stage 26, item 96; decisions 341 and 345, the owner's in chat, and 358, as recommended, all 2026-11-03). In the owner's words: typed notes should "silently expand the information in the task's workspace", and "if a heading is specified in the capture window, then apply the note using the specified heading. Otherwise, use the date as the heading". No schema change and no new package.

- **A note of text alone is no capture** (`save.save_capture`'s `page_note`). A Note on a picked task whose content is typed text only (no file, email, link or summary; not with New task, Update or meeting notes; not made on a page, nor Make it a task) keeps no capture and writes no `capture.md`. Its text goes straight onto the task's page (`edits.add_note`) inside Save's own transaction, so a save that fails keeps nothing and the window keeps the text. It's the owner's own edit of the page: the day's version, a Notes edited line in Activity, search and `Task.md`. A file, an email, a link or a summary makes a note a capture still, a record of what arrived. A note with no task picked stays an unfiled capture, and goes on its task's page when it's filed (decision 358).
- **Where it goes** (`notes_page.place_note`). Each of the note's headings (Ctrl+Alt+1 to 3) goes under the page's heading with the same words, case and spacing aside, the last on the page when it has two, at the end of that heading's section. When the page has no such heading, the note's heading is made at the page's end at the level typed. Lines under none of the note's headings go under the day's heading, at the end of the day's lines, made the first time that day as a capture's day heading is (`add_block`). `note_markup` is `capture_markup` without the capture, so a note's text reads on the page as a capture's did: names as written, file lines out, a `#` heading naming only the page's task left out (release 5.32).
- **Its items** (`_save_items`, now with `from_notes`) are made as before: each task, note, update or decision a capture of its own, on its tasks' pages, with no notes behind it (`from_notes` empty, so its caption doesn't say "from the notes of"). What a parameter or a key date fills is logged as the capture window's (`via` "Capture window").
- **A note split by `#task`** (release 5.32): each part of text alone goes on its task's page the same way; a part with files is a capture, as before. Save's message says both ("Added to the notes of Installation and Rigging plan, one part per #task. Saved 1 capture: …").
- **Every page has day headings** (`Model.uses_day_headings`). Decision 119's per-task choice goes: Day headings for new captures is gone from the task page's Notes menu (`TaskPane`, `BrowseWindow._notes_menu`) and `edits.edit_task`; `tasks.day_headings` stays in docs.db, unread. Every capture's block (an email, a file, an update, a new task's text) goes under its day's heading, a late one under its own day in date order (113), and the Checklist summary's Add item puts its line under today's heading.
- **The capture window** says where a note will land before Save ("Save adds this to the notes of Installation, under Thu 8 Oct 2026."; `CaptureWindow._lands_under`), a split as "Save splits this by #task: a part on the notes of Installation and a note on Rigging plan with 1 file.", and the picker for a split as "None: split by #task". Save's message: "Added to the notes of Installation, under Thu 8 Oct 2026 and Seals." The log has IDs only: "note on T000008's notes page, no capture". The help for the task picker, the capture types and the content text says so.
- `SPEC.md` 6.11 and `GLOSSARY.md`'s day heading follow.

**Settled while building, for the owner's hand test:**

- **A capture's own headings stay in its block,** under its day: the heading rule is for a note that's no capture. A capture is one block, from its caption, so Discard and Add it again can find it.
- **A note's heading matches a page heading at any level.** One the page hasn't got is made at the page's end at the level typed, so a level 2 heading sits under the page's last level 1 heading: today's date when the note also had lines under no heading.
- **The open notes page:** it saves when it loses the caret, which the capture window takes, so the page in docs.db has the typing before Save adds the note; the task page then shows the page again. Two writers' merging (5.25) still brings in only captures' blocks, which is enough while that holds.
- **No undo of Save:** a note on the page is changed or taken off there, as any typing is, and Page history has the day before.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_page.py` (a note under its day, under the page's headings with the same words, and every page's day headings, replacing day headings from Edit task), `tests/test_save.py` (no capture for text alone, a capture still with a file, the log line), `tests/test_hash_task.py` (the split's parts on their pages, the window's words), `tests/test_search.py` (a note found on its page). Tests of what a capture does keep their note a capture with a link (`test_formatted.py`, `test_detail.py`, `test_checklists.py`, `test_quick_timing.py`, `test_quick_input.py`'s rereading), and tests of items and parameters read the page and the "Capture window" log (`test_quick_input.py`, `test_key_dates_text.py`, `test_meeting.py`, `test_headings.py`). New: `test_notes_edit.py`'s capture window saving an open page before a note lands (`AppContext.save_open_pages`). The `Task.md` examples in `tests/fixtures/examples.md` gain the sample's day headings, written from DocRepo as since release 5.25. The full run: 2561 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

**Hand-tested by the owner and merged to `main`** (2026-11-05), **on GitHub** (2026-11-05). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-69-test.md`.

## Release 5.68: long notes pages open at once (2026-11-03)

Found by the design workspace's notes workspace sample (`docs/mods/notes-workspace-sample/`, the notes workspace plan's decision 359), which times a year of weekly meetings on DocRepo's own notes page. No schema change, no new package, nothing new to see: a long page just opens without the wait. 5.65 to 5.67 are round 6's.

- **Opening a page** (`NotesEdit.set_page`). The page was written in a line at a time. The editor is as tall as its text, so once it had been shown at a width, Qt laid the whole page out again after every line: a year of weekly meetings (1,508 lines) took 4 to 5 s to open on the task page or the Task view's Notes page tab. The lines and their styling are now one edit (`beginEditBlock` and `endEditBlock`), so Qt lays the page out once, at its end: the same 1,508 lines open in about 0.15 s, and give exactly the same page back. Moving lines (Alt+Shift+Up and Down) was always one edit, so it was never slow.
- **Pasting a table** (`NotesEdit._paste_html_tables`, release 5.27). After the paste's edit, every line of the page was styled again, each a relayout and a step of undo of its own: 2.5 s on the year's page, and one Ctrl+Z took back only the last line's styling, not the table. The styling is now inside the paste's edit: about 0.1 s, and one Ctrl+Z takes the paste back.
- **Measured** on this computer (offscreen, the task pane at 900 px): the year's page opened in 4.8 s before and 0.15 s after, with 1,509 relayouts before and one after; the table pasted on it in 2.5 s before and 0.1 s after. On the corporate computer a long-running meeting's page should open without a pause.

**Tests:** `tests/test_notes_edit.py`: a 320-line page set on an editor already shown is laid out at most twice, and reads out as it went in (it was laid out once for each line before). `tests/test_pictures_tables.py`: a table pasted at the end of a 150-line page is taken back by one Ctrl+Z. Both fail on 5.64 (322 relayouts; the page not as it was). The full run: 2493 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

**Hand-tested by the owner and merged to `main`** (2026-11-03), **on GitHub** (2026-11-04). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-68-test.md`.

## Release 5.67: links that open, a task's links in one place, and a reference field's link (2026-11-03)

Round 6's items 87, 88 and 90 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-6-plan.md`), from the owner's voice memo of 2026-11-03, built from the mock the owner approved the same day (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/links.js` there), with decisions 314 to 317, 320 and 323, all as recommended. It overturns round 1's decision 5 (copy only, 2026-10-20) and changes the rules file. No schema change and no new package.

- **Opening a link (decision 314).** `opener.start_link` hands a web link to Windows' "open", which gives it to the default browser, as clicking a link in Outlook does. Only on the owner's click, and only an http or https address that `links.read_address` keeps, with a host and no name or password in it. Anything else is refused in words. DocRepo still makes no connection: the browser does. `webbrowser` stays banned, nothing uses Qt's `QDesktopServices`, and only `opener.py` calls `os.startfile` (`tests/test_open_links.py` checks the source). An opened link is recorded in `document_uses` as "open", which the table already allows, so there's no schema change. A folder opens in File Explorer, which is what showing it does.
- **Where links open from (decision 315):**
  - the Links group;
  - the task page's Files tab, where a link's name opens it, with Copy link and Details (its page), and a folder's name opens it, with Copy path;
  - a link's page, with **Open link** beside Copy link;
  - the Task view, where Enter opens a link (it copied it), with Copy link beside it and Ctrl+Shift+C;
  - the Documents view, where a double-click opens a link (it copied it);
  - the notes page, with Ctrl+click on an address in the words, or on a link's or folder's line.
- **The notes page's addresses.** Web addresses in the page's words are underlined in the accent colour, as extra selections, so they're shown and never saved, and the page's markup doesn't change. They're marked again a quarter of a second after typing stops. Hovering one gives "Ctrl+click opens it in your browser" and the address, and with Ctrl held the pointer is a hand. A plain click still places the cursor, and on a file line still opens its page.
- **The Links group (decisions 316 and 317, the mock's order).** `docrepo/task_links.py` gathers a task's links, each once:
  1. its reference fields' links;
  2. its pinned link and folder documents;
  3. the rest of them, newest first;
  4. the web addresses in its notes page's words (named by their path's last part, "In a note" before the host).

  The task page shows them after When and Who, or in the rail on a wide page (`Pages.links_group`), five at most. Each row has a mark (`lk:` pictures in `page_view.py`), its name, which opens it with the whole address in its tooltip, its host, and Copy. A subtask's links are on its own page. With more than five, **All n links** opens the Task view on its Link type (`open_task_view(file_type=...)`, `TaskView.show_type`). That button reads **Show link files in the Task view** when some of the links aren't documents, since the Task view lists only documents (decision 323).
- **A reference field's link (decision 320).**
  - **Settings:** reference fields are now a table, each a name and an optional link address, with what a value of 1234 would link to, worked out as it's typed (`settings_view.ReferenceFields`). `Settings.reference_links` holds the addresses, written once one is set. An address must be http or https, with `{}` once at most (`links.check_link_template`). A malformed `reference_links` in settings.json is left out with a warning, not refused.
  - **The link:** `links.reference_link` puts the value, encoded, where `{}` is or at the end.
  - **Where it shows:**
    - Details, where the value is the link, with Copy and Copy link, or **Add a link**, which opens Settings, for a field with no address;
    - first in the Links group;
    - an open button beside the field's box in Edit task, greyed until a number is typed;
    - Task.md's field table, as a Markdown link.

  `Pages` takes the addresses, and Task.md's writers read them from settings.json (`pages.reference_links_of`), as a dozen callers write pages without the settings.
- **Task.md** gets a Links section after Files, each a Markdown link, a folder's as a `file:` address.
- **The words:** the link's page, a link's row in the capture window, Add link, the help text and the file search's message no longer say DocRepo never opens links. They say it never fetches one, and opens it in the browser on a click. `links.NO_TEXT` changed too; documents saved before keep their old note.
- **Not mocked, built as decided:** the notes page's Ctrl+click (the mockup has no notes page). **Different from the mock:** addresses found in the notes page come after the documents rather than among them by date, since a line on the page has no date of its own.

**Tests:** `tests/test_open_links.py` (new) covers:
- what `start_link` hands over and refuses, and that only `opener.py` calls `startfile`;
- reference links and their checks, and Settings keeping, and leaving out, link addresses;
- a task's links in their order;
- the Links group, narrow and in the rail, with each button's words (decision 323), and Details' link and Add a link;
- Task.md's links;
- the page's open, copy and folder links in the browse window, with an address with `%20` handed over exactly;
- All links to the Task view's Link type, a link's page and its Documents row, and the Task view's Enter;
- Settings' table and its refusal, and Edit task's open button;
- the notes page's underlines and Ctrl+click.

Old tests that pinned copy-only now check opening (`test_links.py`, `test_pins_opener.py`), and the Settings test fills the table (`test_browse_window.py`). Checked by eye on Windows' real platform: Installation's page with its Links group, and Settings' reference fields. The full run, with 5.65 and 5.66: 2556 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

**Merged to `main`** (2026-11-04), with 5.65 and 5.66, before the owner's hand test, with `main`'s 5.68 merged into `round6` first: 2558 passed, 1 skipped, none failed. **On GitHub** (2026-11-05). **Not checked yet:** the owner's hand test, and the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-65-and-5-66-test.md`, then `release-5-67-test.md`.

## Release 5.66: links that go in, whatever their kind (2026-11-03)

Round 6's item 89 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-6-plan.md`), from the owner's voice memo of 2026-11-03 ("I'm still getting some errors when I try and upload certain types of links"), with decisions 318, 319 and 321, all as recommended, built without a mock. Which links the owner hit isn't known yet: the plan's table lists what each kind did, tried against the code. No schema change and no new package.

- **A dragged link with `%20` was refused (a bug).** `read_drop` took a dragged address with `QUrl.toString()`, which turns `%20` back into a space, and a link may not have spaces, so dragging most SharePoint and OneDrive document links onto the pinned window said "That isn't a web link". It now takes the address fully encoded, exactly as dragged. With `links`, any address that isn't a file comes through, so a dragged `mailto:` link says why it isn't kept rather than vanishing.
- **One reader for addresses** (`links.read_address`). Add link, drops, pastes and the pinned window's landing row all go through it. Quotes and angle brackets round an address are taken off (Outlook's plain text writes `<https://…>`). A full stop, comma, semicolon, colon, ! or ? after a web link is taken off, and so is a closing bracket with no opening one in the address. `www.` gets `https://`. A `file:` address becomes the path it names (`file:///C:/…`, `file://server/share/…`). In a box for one address, a web link's spaces become `%20` (SharePoint's Copy path has spaces). OneNote's Copy link, a web line and an `onenote:` line, gives its web line (decision 321). Anything else isn't kept, with why in words: "That's a mailto: link. DocRepo keeps web links (http or https), files and folders." It replaces messages made of a fragment of the address, like "page> can't be found."
- **The content text's link lines** (`links.link_of_line`, used by `text.is_file_line` and the capture window's `_sync_paths`) take angle brackets, a full stop after, and `www.` the same way. A line with a space in it is still words ("https://example.com is down"). The mockup's `isLinkLine` in `model.js` changed with it.
- **Folders (decision 318).** A dropped folder, local or on the LAN, or one typed in the landing row, is kept as a document whose original is its path ending in `\` (`links.is_folder`; no schema change). It has no text (`text_status` none, `FOLDER_TEXT`), no saved copy and nothing to extract. Its row is marked Folder and asks "What's in this folder?". It's listed by its own name (`links.folder_name`, or the whole path for a drive or a share), marked Folder on the capture page, the task page's Files, Task.md and a page of its own. In the Task view it's a type of its own, Folder, with the folder icon, and Enter shows it in File Explorer, which opens it. `held_text` and the pinned window count folders ("1 file, 1 folder, 1 link added"). A folder path typed as a line of the content text stays text, since a folder's path matches while a file's path is still being typed.
- **A link pasted with its name (decision 319).** SharePoint's, OneDrive's, Teams' and Outlook's Copy link put the file's name, linked, on the clipboard. The paste kept bold, lists and so on, but not links, so only the name stayed, and nothing said so. Now:
  - A paste that is one link and nothing else (`paste.lone_link`), on an empty line of the full window, goes in as the address, so the line gives it its row, listed by the name it was copied with (`_link_names`). In the pinned window it's added as a dropped link is, with nothing typed.
  - A link inside other pasted words keeps its address after them, "the pump spec <https://…>" (`rich_edit.spell_out_links`, in every RichEdit's formatted paste).
  - Safe Links are unwrapped in both, with email layer 0's `unwrap`.
- **The troubleshooting report** has a new section, "Links the capture window didn't keep": each one's shape (`diagnose.link_shape`: its scheme, and whether it had spaces, brackets, quotes or several lines), never the address.

**Tests:** `tests/test_link_input.py` (new) has each kind of address in the plan's table, what isn't kept and why, content text lines, `file:` addresses and folder names, the dragged `%20` link, drops and the landing row on the pinned window, a folder saved with no text and shown as Folder everywhere, Open on a folder, a link pasted with its name on its own line, with a name over its address's, inside other words, through Safe Links and on the pinned window, and the report's shapes with no address in them. Two tests that pinned the old refusal of folders (`test_pin_window.py`, `test_capture_window.py`) now check a folder is kept, and one message in `test_links.py` has the new words. The full run, with 5.65: 2541 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

## Release 5.65: the # list (2026-11-03)

Round 6's stage 24 (the design workspace's `docs/mods/round-6-plan.md`), from the owner's voice memo of 2026-11-03: items 85 and 86, decisions 310 to 313, all as recommended, built without a mock. No schema change and no new package.

- **The cut-off name (item 85).** The memo's "with only one result, the dropdown is too short to show the whole name". The cause, measured on Windows' real platform: the list was a fixed 320 px wide, and a row with its path is usually wider, so the list put a horizontal scroll bar inside itself. With one row, the list was one row tall, and the bar covered it (a 13 px viewport for a 22 px row). With several rows, it covered part of the last one. The full window's list had it too.
- **The rows (decision 310).** `RefPopup` (`docrepo/ui/items_panel.py`) now paints each row (`_RefDelegate`): the name in the Body font, then its path or aliases in grey in the Caption size, on one baseline. When they don't fit, the grey part is shortened in its middle, down to 48 px, before the name is cut at its end. Part of a row that fits is drawn whole: Qt's eliding wants a little more room than words measure, as release 5.64's `DropButton` found, and `QRect.right()` is a pixel short of the edge, so both would cut words that fit exactly. The list never scrolls sideways. It's as wide as its longest row, from 320 to 480 px, one row tall per row up to 8, with its frame and padding counted as Qt reports them, not a fixed 6 px. It keeps itself in view as it changes size, in the window or, for the pinned window's list, on the screen, going above the cursor when it can't fit under it (`show_options`'s `above`).
- **Into subtasks (item 86, decisions 311 and 312).** A row whose task has open subtasks ends with a chevron. Right, or a click on the chevron, lists them in the tree's order, under a first row naming the task with a back chevron. Enter there picks the task itself. Left, or the back chevron, goes back up, with the task picked. A level's rows have no grey path of their own, since the task heading the list says where they are, but each one's tooltip has the whole path. Right and Left are the list's only when they do something there; otherwise they move the cursor as before. Typing goes back to the search by name, since every change of the cursor shows a new list, and the text changes only on a pick. Picking puts `#Title ` in with the task's ID, as before. The capture window gives the list `subtasks` (`_subtask_options`: open, not discarded, in `children_of` order), and the `@` list has none.
- **`#` alone (decision 313).** `items.options` lists the top-level tasks in the tree's order, all of them (the list scrolls past 8), so the tree can be walked down with Right. Without a Model, the old list stands. The mockup's `items.js` copy of `options` changed with it.
- **Noted, not changed:** a picked task's ID is kept by its name (`ref_ids`), so two different tasks with the same name, picked in one capture (two "Order parts" under two projects), both go to the one picked last. Keeping each `#` by its place in the text would change how `items.scan` and capture.md read names, so it waits for a need.

**Tests:** `tests/test_ref_list.py` (new) has a lone long row whole with no sideways bar, in both windows; the list one row tall per row; `#` alone; Right and Left two levels down and back, in both windows; picking a subtask, with its ID; Enter on the task itself; Right with nothing to go into; typing going back to the search; `@` with no levels; the chevrons clicked; a level of 12 scrolling down, not across; and the list kept inside the window. Checked by eye on Windows' real platform, before and after, in pictures of the list with one result and two levels down.

## Release 5.64: what-ifs (2026-11-03)

Round 5's thread D, item 73, from the mock the owner approved with release 5.40 (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/plancap.js` in the design workspace) and decisions 226 to 228 and 290. A what-if changes the plan on the Timeline page without changing any task, to see what it does to the load, and is applied as a deliberate last step. No schema change and no new package.

- **What one is** (`docrepo/whatif.py`, new). A named list of the things done in it: a task taken off with everything open under it, a bar moved or stretched, a task with no dates given some, a whole parent moved in whole weeks, and a key date moved (decision 227). Each keeps the tasks it touches as they were. They're kept in docs.db's `app_state` (`timeline_whatifs`) until applied or deleted, and DocRepo always starts on the real plan (decision 228). What's read back is checked, as all data is, and anything that isn't a what-if is left out.
- **What one would do is never worked out by hand.** `whatif.picture` makes its changes through `tasks.update_task` and `tasks.set_key_dates`, the calls Apply makes, inside a transaction that's always rolled back, and reads back the tasks, key dates and hours they changed. So what's tied to a task moves with it (release 5.50), a Duration keeps its days and rates their totals, and the load is `work_calendar`'s own, with no hours logic copied. Each thing done is made in a savepoint, so one that can't be made leaves the rest. The browse window works a picture out again only when the what-if or the database has changed (`BrowseWindow.whatif_picture`); `AppContext.rolled_back` keeps the shared Model meanwhile, since `total_changes` counts rolled-back writes. This replaces the design's overlay, which would have missed ties, Durations and rates.
- **A whole parent** (decision 290) moves its open subtasks and their key dates, but not routine subtasks, deadlines, a key date ticked Deadline or a done one. Tasks are moved in tie order, sources first, so a follower set afterwards is already there and no gap is counted twice.
- **On the page:** a What-ifs button beside Plan lists the real plan, each what-if with how many changes it has and how many are out of date, and a box to name a new one. While one is showing, the button is pressed and a strip under the controls names it (the name can be edited there), says "Your tasks haven't changed", counts its changes, those past their deadlines and those out of date, and has Undo, Review and apply, and Back to the real plan. The button's words never change, so the row of controls doesn't move.
- **Take off** is on the line under the chart and, for each task, on the spotlight's card, with Take off for a task's parent when that isn't the top-level task (the mock's). On the real plan it starts a new what-if. A task taken off stays a row, dashed and faded with an "off" tag, so it can be put back from the same places.
- **The drags.** In a what-if, bars, their ends, key dates and names of tasks with no dates drag as in Plan mode, with no Plan (P says so), and a folded or open parent's subtasks' line drags them all, in whole weeks. A moved bar leaves a dashed outline where it is on the real plan, and a deadline passed is red with "past its deadline". Ctrl+Z and Undo take back the last change in the what-if. Double-click doesn't open Quick edit, and Quick edit and Update aren't offered, as they'd change the real task (decision 292).
- **Capacity** shows the what-if's load: the cells, the spotlight and what isn't counted. The sentence says the real plan's beside it: "Over in 5 weeks (8 on the real plan), the worst the week of 19 Apr 2027, by 56 h (62 h)". The team chart outlines the real plan's columns where they differ, and its tooltip says "On the real plan".
- **Review and apply** (decision 226) lists each thing done once, ticked, with what it would do, what tied to it moves with it, hours kept or not, and deadlines passed. One whose task has changed since, or that can't be made, is unticked and can't be ticked, and says why. Apply makes the ticked ones in one transaction through the same calls as the picture, logged as "What-if: " and its name (tied tasks as "Follows …"), and the what-if goes (`edits.apply_whatif`). Keep it as a what-if leaves everything as it was.
- **Deleting** a what-if with changes in it asks first. The Classic look has no what-ifs.
- **A fix on the way:** a drop-down button's words were cut short at exactly their own width ("Real plan" showed as "Real pl…"), since Qt's eliding wants a little more room than the words measure. `DropButton` now cuts only when they don't fit.

**Tests:** `tests/test_whatif.py` (new) has the picture changing nothing in seven tables, ties, a whole parent with a tie inside it moved once, key dates that stay, out of date, Apply making exactly what the picture showed, one change that can't be made, the list kept and read back, and the review's words. `tests/test_timeline_whatif.py` (new) drives the page: Take off from the line and the card, a bar and a whole parent dragged with real mouse events, no Quick edit, P and Ctrl+Z, naming, flipping, keeping, deleting, Review and apply, and a picture worked out once for each change. `tests/test_real_look.py` checks on Windows' real platform that the row of controls stays one line in a what-if too. The full run: 2306 passed, 1 skipped, none failed, and 2491 passed, 1 skipped, with `main`'s 5.62 and 5.63 merged in.

**Hand-tested by the owner and merged to `main`** (2026-11-03), **on GitHub** (2026-11-04). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer, with the design workspace's `handoff/docrepo/release-5-64-test.md`, after its `release-5-40-test.md`.

## Release 5.40.1: a save waits out a brief refusal (2026-11-02)

The `PermissionError` on replacing a file that came and went in test runs since release 5.61 (`[WinError 5] Access is denied`, in `SafeFS.write_text`).

- **The cause:** Windows refuses to replace a file while anything has it open, even for an instant, and Qt's file watcher opens the file it's watching to look at it. DocRepo watches `Config\wheels.jsonc`, so that an edit to it takes effect at once. Saving that one file over and over, 4 to 8% of saves were refused with a watch on it, and none of 1500 with no watch or a watch on its folder alone. Each refusal cleared within 70 ms. Which tests failed moved around, as it depended on timing, but each was a test saving that file.
- **Who it reached:** the tests, which save that file while the app is watching it. DocRepo itself writes the file only as it starts, before the watch begins, so owners' saves weren't failing from this. Antivirus and the search indexer can hold a file the same way, which is why the fix is in the save and not only about this file.
- **Why the watch stays:** on Windows a watch on the folder sees only names changing, so an editor that saves in place, such as Notepad, wouldn't be noticed.
- **The fix:** `safe_fs._replace`, used by all of SafeFS's replaces: on "access denied" it waits and tries again, from 5 ms and doubling to 100 ms, for up to a second, then says so as before. It's how pip replaces files, for the same reason. It changes nothing about where DocRepo may write, and only `PermissionError` is waited out.

**Tests:** `tests/test_safe_fs.py` has the waiting, the giving up with the file as it was and no temporary file left, and that other errors aren't waited out. On Windows, a real one saves a watched file 400 times: with the wait off, 7 of the 400 failed. `tests/test_wheel_window.py` passed five times out of five; on `main` before this it failed in two runs of four. The full run: 2279 passed, 1 skipped, none failed.

## Release 5.40: Timeline and Capacity on one page (round 5, thread D; the visual review's stage V5)

Built from the approved mock (`docs/browse-tool/mockup/plancap.js` in the design workspace), with decisions 228 to 234, 288 to 292 and the review's V5, all as recommended. What-ifs (decisions 226 to 228) come next as a release of their own, so this page can be used and tested first. No schema change and no new package.

- **One page (decision 229).** The sidebar has Timeline, and Capacity's key, the palette's entry and a wheel's `browse.view` lead to it with Capacity open. `docrepo/load.py` (new) says what's in Capacity's half, and `docrepo/ui/capacity_strip.py` (new) paints it. Hours are read, not worked out, through `capacity.week_cells`, so thread A's changes flow through.
- **One axis.** `_Scale.room` is the page's width, so the head, the rows and Capacity's two parts place a day alike, and the room for a scroll bar is kept whether or not one shows. This also ends a slip in every look: with a scroll bar showing, the rows' track was narrower than the head's by the bar's width.
- **A heat strip (decision 230).** A week is a cell with one number where it fits. It's grey in two steps, caution from 80%, red and bold over 100%, and a strong red from 125% (decision 288). A week under 8 px wide becomes a month, showing its worst week.
- **A cell's number (V5).** Percent to start with, or Hours, remembered, with the other in the tooltip.
- **The case (decisions 231 and 289).** One sentence, counting this week onwards, and a team chart whose red is the hours over each person's own capacity, so it shows even when the team's total is under its capacity line.
- **One People control (decision 232).** It narrows the bars and the load together. A group picks its people, and clicking a name in Capacity picks just them.
- **The spotlight (decision 233).** Clicking a week bands it through the chart, keeps the bars behind it strong and fades the rest, and a card over the page lists their hours, biggest first. Nothing moves. Esc, a second click or × clears it. A task on the card is selected, with its parents opened. Selecting a bar outlines the weeks it adds to. The arrows move between cells, Enter or Space picks one, and a tooltip says what's in it.
- **The bars (decision 234).** One slate colour by default (`self.bars` is `one`), with Colour bars by under Display. Not started is a lighter solid, not hatching. A summary under a bar is a 2 px line. A `project` saved before this release gives way once (`bars_v`), since it was only ever the default.
- **The controls.** One row: Earlier, This week and Later; Plan; People; Display (Order by, Colour bars by, Hours, Focus, Show routine and Labels); Legend; and Show with the range at the right end. They're kept between redraws, not rebuilt. Filters in use show as chips, each with its ×. The levels are at the head of the names' column, gone where nothing nests.
- **A line for what's selected (decision 292).** It's always there under the chart, so picking a bar moves nothing, and its tooltip has the rest. It also holds the count of tasks with no dates, which join the chart only while their fold is open (decision 291).
- **The divider.** Capacity takes what its rows need, up to 62% of the page, until it's dragged, when its height is kept. It folds to its head, sentence and all. What Capacity can't count is said under it, with lists that open.
- **The Classic look** keeps the chart alone, its two rows of controls, the card, the hatching and Capacity's own page. `BrowseWindow` no longer goes to its pages by number, since the new look has one fewer.

**Tests:** `tests/test_load.py` and `tests/test_timeline_page.py` are new, and `tests/test_real_look.py` checks on Windows' real platform that the controls fit one row at the usual window and the parts share the axis. The full run on the final code had 2273 passed, 1 skipped and 1 failed: `tests/test_wheel_window.py::test_a_good_save_is_used_at_once`, a `PermissionError` from Windows on replacing `Config\wheels.jsonc` in `SafeFS.write_text`, in the test's first line, before anything on this page runs. It isn't from this release. Run alone, that file passes here, and on `main` without this release it failed in two of four runs, on other tests that write the same file just after the app starts. The same steps with no app running were never refused in 400 rounds, so something in the app holding the file is likelier than another program, as thought at 5.61. Release 5.40.1 found it, and fixes it.

**Not in this release:** what-ifs, and with them Take off on the card, and moving a whole parent. Thread A's 5.48 follows thread D's merge. The review's bars item, "hatching", is done here.

**Hand-tested by the owner, merged to `main` and on GitHub** (2026-11-02). **Not checked yet:** the corporate computer.
## Release 5.57: the look as decided (2026-11-01)

A review of every window against the design workspace's UI playbook (its `docs/ui-design/visual-review.md`) found that several things decided for the visual overhaul never reached the screen. This release is that review's stage V1, changing what shows without changing what anything does. No schema change and no new package. Windows and buttons stay where they were.

- **The Body font everywhere** (`style.apply_look`, `tokens.BODY_FONT_CLASSES`). Every tree, list, table and menu was Segoe UI 9 pt, though decisions 1 and 2 say Segoe UI Variable 10.5 pt, so the data was smaller than the buttons around it. Windows' Qt theme gives those classes fonts of their own, `QApplication.setFont(font)` doesn't replace them, and setting the style sheet puts them back, so they're set for each class after it. A line of a list stays 28 px (`HtmlDelegate.sizeHint`).
- **A focus ring for the keyboard** (`look.FocusRing`, `look.watch_focus`, `RowTree.paintEvent`). Fusion's own frame reached only 1.3 to 1.7:1 on these colours, and icon buttons had none. A real Tab now puts a 2 px accent ring with a 1 px white line inside it on every button and tick box, and round the current row of a list, at 6.76:1 or better. A click shows none: Qt never takes off its mark that focus moved by keyboard, so `watch_focus` takes it off on a mouse press. It's a small widget that lies over the control, made only when focus moves, so it costs nothing when painting or typing. `tokens.qss` turns off Fusion's frame with `outline: none`.
- **Controls line up** (`FlowLayout._place`). It ignored its own margins, so a command bar sat on the pane's edge, and it put each item at the top of its line, so tick boxes rode above combo boxes on every filter bar. It now keeps its margins and centres each item on its line. Subtle buttons are tighter (8 px at the sides), so the command bar still fits on a row.
- **One height for controls in a row** (`tokens.qss`). Inputs and combo boxes were 33 px and spin boxes 36, against 32 for buttons, and they grew a pixel when focused. They're all 32 px now, focused or not, and so are a chip field (Agenda) and the capture types' strip. Icon buttons are the token's 28 px: a style sheet's sizes beat `setFixedSize`, so the sheet now says 28.
- **Titles are Semibold** (`QLabel#paneTitle`, `QLabel#subtitle`, the pages' `h2`).
